g_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}=g_{\tau,\varepsilon}-\int_{0}^{t}g_{\tau,\varepsilon;s}^{2}\,\mathbf{L}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{A}^{4}_{\tau,\varepsilon;1,s}\mathbf{B}_{\varepsilon;s}(V_{\tau,\varepsilon;s})\,\mathrm{d}s.

(2.36)

We fix the parameter
g_{\tau,\varepsilon}=g_{\tau,\varepsilon;t=0}
implicitly by imposing the following renormalization condition

g_{\tau,\varepsilon;t=1}=1/\lambda,

(2.37)

where
\lambda\in(0,1]
is assumed to be sufficiently small. Taking into account the above boundary condition we rewrite the equation for
g_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}
in the following form

g_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}=1/\lambda+\int_{t}^{1}g_{\tau,\varepsilon;s}^{2}\,\mathbf{L}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{A}^{4}_{\tau,\varepsilon;1,s}\mathbf{B}_{\varepsilon;s}(V_{\tau,\varepsilon;s})\,\mathrm{d}s.

(2.38)

It turns out that the asymptotic behavior of

s\mapsto g_{\tau,\varepsilon;s}^{2}\,\mathbf{L}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{A}^{4}_{\tau,\varepsilon;1,s}\mathbf{B}_{\varepsilon;s}(V_{\tau,\varepsilon;s})=g_{\tau,\varepsilon;s}^{2}\,\mathbf{L}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{A}^{4}_{\tau,\varepsilon;1,s}\mathbf{B}_{\varepsilon;s}(U(1/g_{\tau,\varepsilon;s},r_{\tau,\varepsilon;s},z_{\tau,\varepsilon;s})+W_{\tau,\varepsilon;s})

(2.39)

at
s=0
coincides with the asymptotic behavior of

s\mapsto g_{\tau,\varepsilon;s}^{2}\,\mathbf{L}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{A}^{4}_{\tau,\varepsilon;1,s}\mathbf{B}_{\varepsilon;s}(U(1/g_{\tau,\varepsilon;s},0,0))=\mathbf{L}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{A}^{4}_{\tau,\varepsilon;1,s}\mathbf{B}_{\varepsilon;s}(U(1,0,0))

(2.40)

The above claim is justified a posteriori by assuming that the tuple
(g_{\tau,\varepsilon;{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}},r_{\tau,\varepsilon;{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}},z_{\tau,\varepsilon;{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}},W_{\tau,\varepsilon;{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}})
belongs to the set
\mathscr{Y}_{\tau,\varepsilon}
defined below. Moreover, a direct computation yields

\mathbf{L}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{A}^{4}_{\tau,\varepsilon;1,s}\mathbf{B}_{\varepsilon;s}(U(1,0,0))=\beta_{2}\,s^{-1},\qquad\beta_{2}=2(N-1)/\pi,

(2.41)

for
\tau=0
and
\varepsilon=0
. Consequently, neglecting sub-leading corrections, for
\tau=0
and
\varepsilon=0
the asymptotic behavior of
t\mapsto 1/g_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}
at
t=0
coincides with the asymptotic behavior of
t\mapsto\lambda_{t}
, where
\lambda_{t}
was introduced in Def.
2.2
. Note that the asymptotic behavior of the effective coupling constant
1/g_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}
is easiest to determine by studying Eq. (
2.38
) for its inverse
g_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}
. This is one of the reasons why we chose
g_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}
rather than the effective coupling constant
1/g_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}
to be one of the fundamental variables. Note that by the above argument the effective coupling constant
1/g_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}
with
\varepsilon=0
vanishes logarithmically as
t\searrow 0
. Thus, at high energy the behavior of Schwinger functions of the Gross-Neveu model without the UV cutoff should not differ much from the behavior of the Schwinger functions of the free theory. This property is called in the literature the asymptotic freedom and plays a crucial role in our construction. Let us remark that our scale decomposition
G_{\varepsilon;t}
of the propagator
G_{\varepsilon}
satisfies the condition
\dot{G}_{\varepsilon;t}=0
for
t\in(0,\varepsilon]
. As a result,
\mathbf{B}_{\varepsilon;t}({\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}})=0
and
g_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}=g_{\tau,\varepsilon;\varepsilon}
for
t\in[0,\varepsilon]
. Hence, the flow of the effective coupling constant in the model with the UV cutoff
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
halts when the scale parameter
t
reaches the value
\varepsilon
. In particular, the model with the UV cutoff is not asymptotically free and the value of the parameter
g_{\tau,\varepsilon}=g_{\tau,\varepsilon;0}=g_{\tau,\varepsilon;\varepsilon}
of the potential (
1.4
) fixed by the renormalization condition (
2.37
) vanishes logarithmically as
\varepsilon\searrow 0
. As a side remark, we mention that the assumption
\lambda>0
is necessary for the asymptotic freedom of the model without the UV cutoff as for
\lambda<0
the function
t\mapsto\lambda_{t}
blows up at the scale
t=\mathrm{e}^{1/(\lambda\beta_{2})}
, which is called in the literature the Landau pole. Because of the asymptotic freedom the logarithmic corrections, which were ignored in the bound (
2.19
), improve the asymptotic behavior of
t\mapsto\|V^{m,a,\sigma}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}\|_{\mathscr{M}^{m}}
at
t=0
. Actually, taking into account the logarithmic vanishing of the effective coupling constant a direct inspection of perturbative corrections yields the following bound

\|V^{m,a,\sigma}_{t}\|_{\mathscr{M}^{m}}\lesssim\lambda_{\varepsilon\vee t}^{(m/2-1)\vee 1}\,t^{m/2+|a|-2}

(2.42)

uniform in
\tau,\varepsilon\in[0,1]
and
t\in(0,1]
for all
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
a\in\mathbb{A}^{m}
,
\sigma\in\mathbb{G}^{m}
. Using the above bound one shows that

\|\mathbf{E}\mathbf{A}^{m,a,\sigma}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}\mathbf{B}_{\varepsilon;s}(V_{\tau,\varepsilon;s})\|_{\mathscr{M}^{m}}\lesssim\lambda_{s}^{(m/2-1)\vee 2}\,s^{m/2+|a|-3}

(2.43)

uniformly in
\tau,\varepsilon\in[0,1]
and
t,s\in(0,1]
. Hence, using Lemma
2.4
(A) we obtain

\big\|\textstyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{A}^{m,a,\sigma}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u,s}\mathbf{B}_{\varepsilon;s}(V_{\tau,\varepsilon;s})\,\mathrm{d}s\big\|_{\mathscr{M}^{m}}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\lambda_{s}^{(m/2-1)\vee 2}\,s^{m/2+|a|-3}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\lesssim\lambda_{t}^{(m/2-1)\vee 2}\,t^{m/2+|a|-2}

(2.44)

uniform in
\tau,\varepsilon\in[0,1]
and
t,u\in(0,1]
unless
m=4
and
a=0
, or
m=2
and
|a|\leq 1
. By Lemma
2.4
(D) applied with
\rho=1
we have

\big\|\textstyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{A}^{m,a,\sigma}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u,s}\mathbf{B}_{\varepsilon;s}(V_{\tau,\varepsilon;s})\,\mathrm{d}s\big\|_{\mathscr{M}^{m}}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\lambda_{s}^{2}\,s^{-1}\,\mathrm{d}s\lesssim\lambda_{t}

(2.45)

uniformly in
\tau,\varepsilon\in[0,1]
and
t,u\in(0,1]
for
m=4
and
a=0
, or
m=2
and
|a|=1
. If
m=2
and
a=0
, then for
\varepsilon=0
the function
s\mapsto\mathbf{E}\mathbf{A}^{m,a,\sigma}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u,s}\mathbf{B}_{\varepsilon;s}(V_{s})
is not integrable at
s=0
. However, by Lemma
2.4
(C) applied with
\rho=2
,
\varrho=-1
and
\lambda\in(0,1]
small enough we have

\big\|\textstyle\int_{t}^{1}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{A}^{m,a,\sigma}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u,s}\mathbf{B}_{\varepsilon;s}(V_{\tau,\varepsilon;s})\,\mathrm{d}s\big\|_{\mathscr{M}^{m}}\lesssim\int_{t}^{1}\lambda_{s}^{2}\,s^{-2}\,\mathrm{d}s\lesssim\lambda_{t}^{2}\,t^{-1}

(2.46)

uniformly in
\tau,\varepsilon\in[0,1]
and
t,u\in(0,1]
for
m=2
and
a=0
. The bound (
2.45
) suggests that Eq. (
2.32
) for
z_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}
is well-posed for
\varepsilon=0
. On the other hand, Eq. (
2.31
) for
r_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}
is not expected to be well-posed for
\varepsilon=0
. To address the problem with Eq. (
2.31
) we impose the following renormalization condition

r_{\tau,\varepsilon;t=1}=0

(2.47)

that fixes implicitly the mass counterterm
r_{\tau,\varepsilon}=r_{\tau,\varepsilon;t=0}
and rewrite this equation so that it involves the integral appearing on the LHS of the bound (
2.46
). Using Eq. (
2.31
) we obtain the following equation

r_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}=-\mathbf{L}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{A}^{2}_{\tau,\varepsilon;1,t}U(1/g_{\tau,\varepsilon;t},0,0)-\int_{t}^{1}\mathbf{L}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{A}^{2}_{\tau,\varepsilon;1,s}\mathbf{B}_{\varepsilon;s}(V_{\tau,\varepsilon;s})\,\mathrm{d}s.

(2.48)

Let us remark that since we do not impose any non-trivial renormalization condition for the parameter
z_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}
introducing this parameter in the ansatz (
2.24
) is not necessary and is done mainly because it is convenient when proving estimates.

The claim is that the system of equations (
2.38
), (
2.48
),
(
2.32
), (
2.33
),
(
2.34
) for the tuple
(g_{\tau,\varepsilon;{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}},r_{\tau,\varepsilon;{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}},z_{\tau,\varepsilon;{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}},W_{\tau,\varepsilon;{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}})
remains well-posed in the limit
\tau,\varepsilon\searrow 0
. Since we would like to construct a solution of this system of equations using the contraction principle we have to first find an appropriate complete metric space of tuples
(g_{\tau,\varepsilon;{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}},r_{\tau,\varepsilon;{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}},z_{\tau,\varepsilon;{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}},W_{\tau,\varepsilon;{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}})
. We will define this metric space as a closed subset of a certain Banach space. The norm in the Banach space has to control the dependence of
g_{\tau,\varepsilon;t},r_{\tau,\varepsilon;t},z_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}\in\mathbb{R}
on
t\in(0,1]
as well as the dependence of
\|W^{m,a,\sigma}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}\|_{\mathscr{M}^{m}}
on
t\in(0,1]
and
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
a\in\mathbb{A}^{m}
,
\sigma\in\mathbb{G}^{m}
. To this end, for
\alpha,\beta\in[1,\infty)
and
\gamma\in[0,\infty)
we introduce the Banach space of functionals
s\mapsto W_{s}=(W^{m,a,\sigma}_{s})_{m\in\mathbb{N}_{+},a\in\mathbb{A}^{m},\sigma\in\mathbb{G}^{m}}
depending on the scale parameter
s\in(0,1]
equipped with the following norm

\|W_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\|_{\mathscr{V}^{\alpha,\beta;\gamma}}:=\sup_{m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}\alpha^{m}m^{\beta}\,\|W^{m}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\|_{\mathscr{V}^{m;\gamma}},

(2.49)

where

\|W^{m}_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\|_{\mathscr{V}^{m;\gamma}}:=\sum_{a\in\mathbb{A}^{m}}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathbb{G}^{m}}\sup_{s\in(0,1]}\lambda_{s}^{-\rho_{\gamma,\kappa}(m)}\,s^{2-m/2-|a|}\,\|w^{m}_{s}W^{m,a,\sigma}_{s}\|_{\mathscr{M}^{m}}.

(2.50)

The weight
w_{s}^{m}
depending only on the relative coordinates and growing stretched exponentially is needed to establish stretched exponential decay of truncated correlations as well as to prove the desired estimates for the map
\mathbf{R}
introduced above. The bound (
2.44
) suggest that the kernels of a solution
W_{\tau,\varepsilon;{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}}
of Eqs. (
2.33
) and (
2.34
) should have finite norms
\|W_{\tau,\varepsilon;{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}}^{m}\|_{\mathscr{V}^{m;\gamma}}
defined in terms of
\rho_{\gamma,\kappa}(m)=(m/2-1)\vee 2
. In order to give oneself some wiggle room when proving estimates we choose instead
\rho_{\gamma,\kappa}(m):=\gamma+2\kappa m
with
\gamma=2-80\kappa
, where
\kappa=1/1000
was fixed in Def.
2.1
. It turns out the kernels of the functional
V_{\tau,\varepsilon;{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}}
related to
(g_{\tau,\varepsilon;{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}},r_{\tau,\varepsilon;{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}},z_{\tau,\varepsilon;{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}},W_{\tau,\varepsilon;{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}})
by Eq. (
2.24
) have finite norms
\|t\mapsto\theta(t-\varepsilon)\,V_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}^{m}\|_{\mathscr{V}^{m;\gamma}}
with
\gamma=1-40\kappa
. The presence of the function
\theta(t-\varepsilon)
in the above expression is needed because in the model with the UV cutoff the flow of the effective coupling constant
1/g_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}
halts at
t=\varepsilon
and consequently
1/g_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}
does not vanish in the limit
t\searrow 0
if
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
. Recall that
\dot{G}_{\varepsilon;t}=0
and
\mathbf{B}_{\varepsilon;t}({\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}})=0
if
t\in(0,\varepsilon]
. As a result, the RHS of Eqs. (
2.38
), (
2.48
),
(
2.32
), (
2.33
),
(
2.34
) depend only on
t\mapsto\theta(t-\varepsilon)\,V_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}
. The usefulness of the norm
\|{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\|_{\mathscr{V}^{\alpha,\beta;\gamma}}
comes from the following estimates

\big\|s\mapsto s\,\mathbf{B}_{\varepsilon;s}(V_{s})\big\|_{\mathscr{V}^{\alpha,\beta-1;2\gamma}}\leq C\,\|s\mapsto V_{s}\|_{\mathscr{V}^{\alpha,\beta;\gamma}}^{2}

(2.51)

and

\big\|t\mapsto\textstyle\int_{0}^{t}\Pi_{>4}V_{s}/s\,\mathrm{d}s\big\|_{\mathscr{V}^{\alpha,\beta;\gamma}}\leq C\,\|s\mapsto V_{s}\|_{\mathscr{V}^{\alpha,\beta-1;\gamma}}

(2.52)

valid for
\alpha\in[1,\infty)
,
\beta\in(2,\infty)
,
\gamma\in[0,\infty)
, where
C\in(0,\infty)
is a universal constant and given a functional
V=(V^{m,a,\sigma})_{m\in\mathbb{N}_{+},a\in\mathbb{A}^{m},\sigma\in\mathbb{G}^{m}}
we define the functional
\Pi_{>4}V
by the equalities
(\Pi_{>4}V)^{m,a,\sigma}=0
if
m\leq 4
and
(\Pi_{>4}V)^{m,a,\sigma}=V^{m,a,\sigma}
if
m>4
. The presence of the operator
\Pi_{>4}
in the second of the above estimates is related to the fact that the relevant kernels of
s\mapsto\mathbf{B}_{\varepsilon;s}(V_{s})
are not absolutely integrable at
s=0
and need a special treatment. Observe also that since the scale dependent functional
s\mapsto V_{s}-\Pi_{>4}V_{s}
has only finitely many non-zero kernels the parameters
\alpha
and
\beta
do not play any role when estimating its
\|{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\|_{\mathscr{V}^{\alpha,\beta;\gamma}}
norm. The estimates (
2.51
) and (
2.52
) imply in particular that

\big\|t\mapsto\textstyle\int_{0}^{t}\Pi_{>4}\mathbf{B}_{\varepsilon;s}(V_{s})\,\mathrm{d}s\big\|_{\mathscr{V}^{\alpha,\beta;2\gamma}}\leq C\,\|s\mapsto V_{s}\|_{\mathscr{V}^{\alpha,\beta;\gamma}}^{2}.

(2.53)

The remarkable property of the above bound is the fact that the norms on both sides of this bound have the same parameter
\alpha
and
\beta
. Recall that the RHS of Eq. (
2.33
) involves
\mathbf{E}\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}\mathbf{B}_{\varepsilon;s}(V_{\tau,\varepsilon;s})
. In particular, to be able to solve the above system in the space
\mathscr{V}^{\alpha,\beta;\gamma}
of functionals
W_{\tau,\varepsilon;{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}}
the validity of an estimate of the form

\|t\mapsto\,\textstyle\int_{0}^{t}\Pi_{>4}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}V_{s}/s\,\mathrm{d}s\|_{\mathscr{V}^{\alpha,\beta;\gamma}}\leq C\,\|s\mapsto V_{s}\|_{\mathscr{V}^{\alpha,\beta-1;\gamma}}\qquad\mathrm{(false)}

(2.54)

with a universal constant
C\in(0,\infty)
appears to be crucial. Indeed, the bounds (
2.51
) and (
2.54
) imply the bound

\big\|t\mapsto\textstyle\int_{0}^{t}\Pi_{>4}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}\mathbf{B}_{\varepsilon;s}(V_{s})\,\mathrm{d}s\big\|_{\mathscr{V}^{\alpha,\beta;2\gamma}}\leq C\,\|s\mapsto V_{s}\|_{\mathscr{V}^{\alpha,\beta;\gamma}}^{2}\qquad\mathrm{(false)}

(2.55)

with a universal constant
C\in(0,\infty)
, which would allow to control the
\|{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\|_{\mathscr{V}^{\alpha,\beta;\gamma}}
norm of the RHS of Eq. (
2.33
). Recall that the definition of the map
\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}
involves the increment
\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}
of the scale decomposition of the free field. Using the bound

|\mathbf{E}(\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}^{\sigma_{1}}(x_{1})\ldots\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}^{\sigma_{k}}(x_{k}))|\leq\sup_{\sigma\in\mathbb{G}}\|\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}^{\sigma}\|^{k}_{\mathscr{C}}

(2.56)

where
\|\phi\|_{\mathscr{C}}:=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}\|\phi(x)\|_{\mathscr{B}(\mathscr{H})}
, as well as a similar bound for
\partial^{a}\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}^{\sigma}
one shows, with some effort, that the bound (
2.54
) would be true for sufficiently small
\lambda\in(0,1]
if the following bound

\|\partial^{a}\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}^{\sigma}\|_{\mathscr{C}}\leq c\,(s^{-1/2-|a|}-t^{-1/2-|a|}),\qquad 0<s\leq t\leq 1,\qquad\mathrm{(false)}

(2.57)

with a universal constant
c\in(0,\infty)
was true. The above estimate would imply in particular that the scale decomposition of the free field is locally Lipschitz continuous. However, the scale decomposition of the free field should have similar regularity in the scale parameter, which plays the role of time, to the Grassmann Brownian motion, which is only Hölder continuous with exponent
1/2
. In fact, the following bound

\|\partial^{a}\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}^{\sigma}\|_{\mathscr{C}}\leq c\,(s^{-1-2|a|}-t^{-1-2|a|})^{1/2},\qquad 0<s\leq t\leq 1,

(2.58)

holds true with a universal constant
c\in(0,\infty)
. This indicates that the bound (
2.54
) is false. The above argument is obviously not conclusive. However, closer analysis reveals that it is unlikely that the estimate (
2.55
) is true. We refer the reader to
[
SW00
]
for related comments. As an aside, we mention that as observed in
[
SW00
]
the proof of estimates for fermionic correlations given in
[
BW88
,
BW99
]
is not correct precisely because the scale decomposition of the free field is not Lipschitz continuous. This suggest that, in a sense, the difficulty we face is related to the fact that the scale decomposition of the free field is only
1/2
Hölder continuous. Note also that in the discrete renormalization group one studies scales
t,s\in(0,1]
such that
s=t/L
with
L\in(1,\infty)
fixed. For scales
t,s\in(0,1]
satisfying the above relation the estimates (
2.57
) and (
2.58
) with some constants
c\in(0,\infty)
are equivalent.

One of the novel ideas of this work is the use of the following norm

\|W_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\|_{\mathscr{W}^{\alpha,\beta;\gamma}_{\tau,\varepsilon}}:=\sup_{u\in[0,1]}\|s\mapsto\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u\vee s,s}W_{s}\|_{\mathscr{V}^{\alpha,\beta;\gamma}}

(2.59)

in the space of scale dependent functionals
s\mapsto W_{s}=(W^{m,a,\sigma}_{s})_{m\in\mathbb{N}_{+},a\in\mathbb{A}^{m},\sigma\in\mathbb{G}^{m}}
. Since
\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;s,s}V_{s}=V_{s}
the norm
\|{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\|_{\mathscr{W}^{\alpha,\beta;\gamma}_{\tau,\varepsilon}}
is stronger than
\|{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\|_{\mathscr{V}^{\alpha,\beta;\gamma}}
. We stress that the norm
\|{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\|_{\mathscr{W}^{\alpha,\beta;\gamma}_{\tau,\varepsilon}}
depends on the cutoffs
\tau,\varepsilon\in(0,1]
. We also note that the functional
\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u\vee s,s}V_{s}
, which appears in the definition of the norm
\|{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\|_{\mathscr{W}^{\alpha,\beta;\gamma}_{\tau,\varepsilon}}
, takes values in
\mathscr{F}_{u\vee s,s}\subset\mathscr{F}\subset\mathscr{B}(\mathscr{H})
. For this reason we are forced to work with kernels that are measures valued in a Banach space
\mathscr{B}(\mathscr{H})
. The main advantage of the norm
\|{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\|_{\mathscr{W}^{\alpha,\beta;\gamma}_{\tau,\varepsilon}}
over
\|{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\|_{\mathscr{V}^{\alpha,\beta;\gamma}}
is the validity of the following estimate

\big\|t\mapsto{\textstyle\int_{0}^{t}}\Pi_{>4}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}\mathbf{B}_{\varepsilon;s}(V_{s})\,\mathrm{d}s\big\|_{\mathscr{W}^{\alpha,\beta;2\gamma}_{\tau,\varepsilon}}\leq C\,\|s\mapsto V_{s}\|_{\mathscr{W}^{\alpha,\beta;\gamma}_{\tau,\varepsilon}}^{2},

(2.60)

where
C\in(0,\infty)
is some universal constant. As we argued above an analogous estimate is almost certainly false if the norm
\|{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\|_{\mathscr{W}^{\alpha,\beta;\gamma}_{\tau,\varepsilon}}
is replaced by the more standard norm
\|{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\|_{\mathscr{V}^{\alpha,\beta;\gamma}}
. Let us stress that the lack of a norm in the space of scale dependent functionals for which the estimate of the form (
2.60
) holds true was the main obstacle in the construction of Grassmann measures of fermionic quantum field theories with the use of the Polchinski flow equation. Since the estimate (
2.60
) is of crucial importance let us sketch the main ideas behind its proof. First, observe that in order to prove the estimate (
2.60
) we have to control the
\|{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\|_{\mathscr{V}^{\alpha,\beta;2\gamma}_{\tau,\varepsilon}}
norm of the functional
\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u,t}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}\mathbf{B}_{\varepsilon;s}(V_{s})
in terms of the
\|{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\|_{\mathscr{V}^{\alpha,\beta;\gamma}_{\tau,\varepsilon}}
norm of the functional
\mathbf{A}^{m}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u,s}V_{s}
. Next, note that for
0<s\leq t\leq u\leq 1
it holds

\begin{gathered}\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u,s}V=\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u,t}\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}V,\qquad\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u,s}\mathbf{B}_{\varepsilon;s}(V)=\mathbf{B}_{\varepsilon;s}(\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u,s}V),\\
\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u,t}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}V=\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u,t}\mathbf{E}_{t}\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}V=\mathbf{E}_{t}\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u,t}\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}V=\mathbf{E}_{t}\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u,s}V.\end{gathered}

(2.61)

The identities in the first line above follow easily from the definition (
2.16
) of the maps
\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}
and
\mathbf{B}_{\varepsilon;s}
. The first equality in the second line is a consequence of the fact that the kernels of the functional
\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}V
take values in
\mathscr{F}_{t,s}
and on
\mathscr{F}_{t,s}\subset\mathscr{F}_{t,0}
the expected value
\mathbf{E}
coincides with the conditional expectation
\mathbf{E}_{t}
. To prove the second equality in the second line we use the fact that the conditional expectation
\mathbf{E}_{t}
act trivially on
\mathscr{F}_{u,t}\subset\mathscr{F}_{1,t}
. As a result, the following identity

\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u,t}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}\mathbf{B}_{\varepsilon;s}(V)=\mathbf{E}_{t}\mathbf{B}_{\varepsilon;s}(\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u,s}V)

(2.62)

holds true. We also note that
\|\mathbf{E}_{t}V\|_{\mathscr{M}^{m}}\leq\|V\|_{\mathscr{M}^{m}}
and

\|u\mapsto\mathbf{E}_{t}V_{u}\|_{\mathscr{V}^{\alpha,\beta;\gamma}}\leq\|u\mapsto V_{u}\|_{\mathscr{V}^{\alpha,\beta;\gamma}}.

(2.63)

Consequently, ignoring the presence of the operator
\Pi_{>4}
in the estimate (
2.53
) we obtain

\big\|t\mapsto{\textstyle\int_{0}^{t}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}\mathbf{B}_{\varepsilon;s}(V_{s})\,\mathrm{d}s\big\|_{\mathscr{W}^{\alpha,\beta;2\gamma}_{\tau,\varepsilon}}=\sup_{u\in(0,1]}\big\|t\mapsto\textstyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u,t}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}\mathbf{B}_{\varepsilon;s}(V_{s})\,\mathrm{d}s\big\|_{\mathscr{V}^{\alpha,\beta;2\gamma}}\\
=\sup_{u\in(0,1]}\big\|t\mapsto{\textstyle\int_{0}^{t}}\mathbf{E}_{t}\mathbf{B}_{\varepsilon;s}(\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u,s}V_{s})\,\mathrm{d}s\big\|_{\mathscr{V}^{\alpha,\beta;2\gamma}}\leq\sup_{u\in(0,1]}\big\|t\mapsto\textstyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathbf{B}_{\varepsilon;s}(\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u,s}V_{s})\,\mathrm{d}s\big\|_{\mathscr{V}^{\alpha,\beta;2\gamma}}\\
\leq C\,\sup_{u\in(0,1]}\|s\mapsto\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u,s}V_{s}\|_{\mathscr{V}^{\alpha,\beta;\gamma}}^{2}=C\,\|s\mapsto V_{s}\|_{\mathscr{W}^{\alpha,\beta;\gamma}_{\tau,\varepsilon}}^{2}.

(2.64)

The first and the last equality above follow from the definition of the norm
\|{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\|_{\mathscr{W}^{\alpha,\beta;\gamma}_{\tau,\varepsilon}}
. The second equality above follows from the identity (
2.62
). The first bound above follows from the estimate (
2.63
). Finally, to prove the second bound above we used the estimate (
2.53
) with the operator
\Pi_{>4}
omitted. Since the presence of the operator
\Pi_{>4}
in the estimate (
2.53
) is crucial the above reasoning is of course not correct. However, a more complicated argument not ignoring the presence of the operator
\Pi_{>4}
in the estimate (
2.53
) allows to establish rigorously the desired bound (
2.60
).

We are ready to define the complete metric space in which we will solve the system of equations (
2.38
), (
2.48
),
(
2.32
), (
2.33
),
(
2.34
). Let us first introduce the Banach space of tuples

X_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\equiv(g_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}},r_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}},z_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}},W_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}})\in C((0,1],\mathbb{C})\times C((0,1],\mathbb{C})\times C((0,1],\mathbb{C})\times\mathscr{W}^{8,4;2-80\kappa}_{\tau,\varepsilon}=:\mathscr{X}_{\tau,\varepsilon}

(2.65)

equipped with the norm

\|X_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\|:=\sup_{t\in(0,1]}\lambda_{t}^{\kappa_{1}+1}\,|g_{t}|+\sup_{t\in(0,1]}\lambda_{t}^{\kappa_{2}-1}\,t\,|r_{t}|+\sup_{t\in(0,1]}\lambda_{t}^{\kappa_{3}-1}\,|z_{t}|+\|W_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\|_{\mathscr{W}^{8,4;2-80\kappa}_{\tau,\varepsilon}},

(2.66)

where
\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2},\kappa_{3}\in(0,1)
are certain small parameters. Next, for
\tau,\varepsilon\in[0,1]
we define the complete metric space

\mathscr{Y}_{\tau,\varepsilon}:=\{X_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\in\mathscr{X}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\,|\,\|X_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\|_{\mathscr{X}_{\tau,\varepsilon}}\leq 1,~\forall_{t\in(0,1]}\,\mathrm{Im}\,g_{t}=\mathrm{Im}\,r_{t}=\mathrm{Im}\,z_{t}=0,\,\lambda_{\varepsilon\vee t}\,g_{t}\geq\lambda^{\kappa}\}

(2.67)

and a map

\mathscr{Y}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\ni X_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\mapsto\mathbf{X}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}(X_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}):=(\mathbf{g}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}(X_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}),\mathbf{z}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}(X_{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}})),\qquad t\in(0,1],

(2.68)

such that a fixed point of
\mathbf{X}_{\tau,\varepsilon;{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}}
coincides with a solution of the system of equations (

), (

),
(

), (

),

). In particular, for all

it holds

and

. We prove that there exists

such that for all

and

. We omit

and

if

and
\varepsilon=0
. In order to establish convergence of the Schwinger functions as

we have to control

. To this end, we introduce a certain norm

in

weaker than

and prove that

\lim_{\tau,\varepsilon\searrow 0}\|X_{{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}}-X_{\tau,\varepsilon;{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}}\|_{\tilde{\mathscr{X}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}}=0.

(2.69)

The norm
\|(g_{{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}},r_{{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}},z_{{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}},W_{{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}})\|_{\tilde{\mathscr{X}}_{\tau,\varepsilon}}
is defined in terms of a norm
\|W_{{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}}\|_{\tilde{\mathscr{W}}^{2,3;2-80\kappa}_{\tau,\varepsilon}}
that has a form similar to the norm

but involves a different weight. The use of a different weight having some decay at infinity is crucial for the existence of the infinite volume limit
\tau\searrow 0
. Let us also mention that the norm
\|{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\|_{\tilde{\mathscr{W}}^{2,3;2-80\kappa}_{\tau,\varepsilon}}
is stronger than a certain norm
\|{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\|_{\tilde{\mathscr{V}}^{2,3;2-80\kappa}}
independent of
\tau,\varepsilon\in(0,1]
whose definition is very similar to the definition of the norm
\|{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\|_{\mathscr{V}^{2,3;2-80\kappa}}
but involves a different weight.

Finally, let us discuss the convergence of the Schwinger functions as

. To this end, first recall that the fixed point

of the map

of the flow equation (

) and

is related by Eqs. (
2.11
), (

) and (

) to a solution

of the flow equation (
2.10
). We conclude that the functional
U_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}
is an effective potential at the scale
t\in(0,1]
, that is
U_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}
satisfies Eq. (
2.7
), and
U_{\tau,\varepsilon;t=1}
is related by Eq. (
2.9
) to the generating functional of the Schwinger functions. The convergence of the Schwinger functions as
\tau,\varepsilon\searrow 0
follows now from the existence of the limit (
2.69
).

Lemma 2.4
.

For all
\rho,\varrho\in\mathbb{R}
and
\eta\in(0,1)
there exists
C\in(0,\infty)
such that the following statements are true for all
\lambda\in(0,1]
and
t\in(0,1]
.

(A)

If
\rho\geq 0
and
\varrho>0
, then
\int_{0}^{t}\lambda_{s}^{\rho}\,s^{\varrho-1}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq\lambda_{t}^{\rho}\,t^{\varrho}/\varrho
.

(B)

If
\rho\geq 0
and
\varrho>0
, then
\int_{0}^{t}\lambda_{s}^{\rho}\,s^{\varrho-1}\,(1-s/t)^{-\eta}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\,\lambda_{t}^{\rho}\,t^{\varrho}
.

(C)

If
\rho\geq 0
and
\varrho<-\beta_{2}\,\rho\,\lambda
, then
\int_{t}^{1}\lambda_{s}^{\rho}\,s^{\varrho-1}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\,\lambda_{t}^{\rho}\,t^{\varrho}
.

(D)

If
\rho>0
, then
\int_{0}^{t}\lambda_{s}^{\rho+1}\,s^{-1}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\,\lambda_{t}^{\rho}
.

(E)

If
\rho<0
, then
\int_{t}^{1}\lambda_{s}^{\rho+1}\,s^{-1}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\,\lambda_{t}^{\rho}
.

Remark 2.5
.

Recall that
\lambda_{t}
and
\beta_{2}
were introduced in Def.
2.2
.

Proof.

For
\rho\in[0,\infty)
and
\varrho\in(0,\infty)
we have

\int_{0}^{t}\lambda_{s}^{\rho}\,s^{\varrho-1}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq\lambda_{t}^{\rho}\int_{0}^{t}s^{\varrho-1}\,\mathrm{d}s=\lambda_{t}^{\rho}\,t^{\varrho}/\varrho

(2.70)

as well as

\int_{0}^{t}\lambda_{s}^{\rho}\,s^{\varrho-1}\,(1-s/t)^{-\eta}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq\lambda_{t}^{\rho}\int_{0}^{t}s^{\varrho-1}\,(1-s/t)^{-\eta}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq\lambda_{t}^{\rho}\,t^{\varrho}\int_{0}^{1}s^{\varrho-1}\,(1-s)^{-\eta}\,\mathrm{d}s.

(2.71)

This proves the bounds (A) and (B). To prove the bound (C) we use the fact that for
\rho\in[0,\infty)
and
\varrho\in(-\infty,-\beta_{2}\,\rho\,\lambda)
the following estimates

\int_{t}^{1}\lambda_{s}^{\rho}\,s^{\varrho-1}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq(-\varrho-\beta_{2}\,\rho\,\lambda)^{-1}\int_{t}^{1}(-\varrho-\beta_{2}\,\rho\,\lambda_{s})\,\lambda_{s}^{\rho}\,s^{-\varrho-1}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq(-\varrho-\beta_{2}\,\rho\,\lambda)^{-1}\int_{t}^{1}\partial_{s}(-\lambda_{s}^{\rho}\,s^{\varrho})\,\mathrm{d}s\leq\lambda_{t}^{\rho}\,t^{\varrho}/(-\varrho-\beta_{2}\,\rho\,\lambda)

(2.72)

hold true. The bound (D) is a consequence of the equalities

\int_{0}^{t}\lambda_{s}^{\rho+1}\,s^{-1}\,\mathrm{d}s=\beta_{2}^{-1}\int_{0}^{t}\lambda_{s}^{\rho-1}\,\mathrm{d}\lambda_{s}=\lambda_{t}^{\rho}/(\beta_{2}\rho)

(2.73)

valid for
\rho\in(0,\infty)
and the bound (E) is a consequence of the estimates

\int_{t}^{1}\lambda_{s}^{\rho+1}\,s^{-1}\,\mathrm{d}s=\beta_{2}^{-1}\int_{t}^{1}\lambda_{s}^{\rho-1}\,\mathrm{d}\lambda_{s}\leq\lambda_{t}^{\rho}/(-\beta_{2}\rho)

(2.74)

valid for
\rho\in(-\infty,0)
. This finishes the proof.
∎

The rest of this article is organised as follows. In Sec.
3
we define the spaces of distributions and functionals on a torus and introduce the notation used in the paper. Sec.
4
contains the definition of the Berezin integral and the Grassmann measure
\mu_{\tau,\varepsilon}
of the Gross-Neveu model with cutoffs. In Sec.
5
we define the scale decomposition
G_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}
of the propagator
G_{\tau,\varepsilon}
and discuss its properties. In Sec.
6
we introduce the filtered non-commutative probability space
(\mathscr{F},\mathbf{E})
of the spacetime white noise
\xi
and define the conditional expected value
\mathbf{E}_{t}
as well as the scale decomposition of the free field
\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}
. In Sec.
7
we present the equation for an effective potential and examine its relation with the Polchinski equation. In Sec.
8
we discuss the symmetries of the Gross-Neveu model. In Sec.
9
we define the weights. Sec.
10
contains the definitions of the spaces
\mathscr{V}^{m;\gamma}
,
\mathscr{V}^{\alpha,\beta;\gamma}
,
\mathscr{W}^{\alpha,\beta;\gamma}_{\tau,\varepsilon}
of kernels and functionals in infinite volume. In Sec.
11
we introduce the decomposition of kernels into the local part and the remainder and discuss its properties. In Sec.
12
we establish bounds for the maps
\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}
and
\mathbf{B}_{\varepsilon;t}
. In Sec.
13
we introduce the space
\mathscr{X}_{\tau,\varepsilon}
and the map
\mathbf{X}_{\tau,\varepsilon}
and prove that the map
\mathbf{X}_{\tau,\varepsilon}
is a contraction. In Sec.
14
we prove that the fixed point of the map
\mathbf{X}_{\tau,\varepsilon}
yields a solution of the Polchinski equation. In Sec.
15
we show that the effective potential at unit scale is directly related to the generating functional of the Schwinger functions and prove Theorem
1.1
stated in the introduction. The core of the proof of the main result is contained in Sec.
13
,
14
and
15
.

3
Distributions and functionals on torus

In this section we introduce the basic notation we use throughout the paper. In particular, we define the spaces of distributions and functionals on a torus.

Definition 3.1
.

For
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
we denote by
\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2m})
and
\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{2m})
the space of Schwartz functions and distributions, respectively. We denote by
\langle V,\phi\rangle\in\mathbb{C}
the standard paring between a distribution
V\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{2m})
and a test function
\phi\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2m})
. By
\ast
we mean the convolution in
\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{2m})
. We use the following convention for the Fourier transform

(\mathbf{F}f)(p):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2m}}f(x)\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}p\cdot x}\,\mathrm{d}x,\qquad f(x)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2m}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2m}}(\mathbf{F}f)(p)\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}p\cdot x}\,\mathrm{d}p.

(3.1)

Definition 3.2
.

Let
\tau\in(0,1]
. We define
\mathbb{T}^{2}_{\tau}:=(\mathbb{R}/\tau^{-1}\mathbb{Z})^{2}
. Occasionally, we identify
\mathbb{T}^{2}_{\tau}
with the set
(-1/(2\tau),1/(2\tau))^{2}\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
. For
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
we denote by
\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2m})
the subspace of
\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{2m})
consisting of distributions that are
1/\tau
periodic. Given
\varphi\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
we define
\mathbf{P}_{\tau}\varphi\in L^{1}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})
by the equality

\mathbf{P}_{\tau}\varphi=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\varphi({\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}+n/\tau).

(3.2)

For
V\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2m})
and
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2m})
we write

\langle V,\phi\rangle_{\tau}:=\langle V,\chi_{\tau}^{\otimes m}\phi\rangle\in\mathbb{C},

(3.3)

where
\chi_{\tau}\in C^{\infty}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
is such that
\mathbf{P}_{\tau}\chi_{\tau}=1
. By
\ast_{\tau}
we mean the convolution in
\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2m})
. We use the following convention for the Fourier transform

(\mathbf{F}_{\tau}f)(p):=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2m}_{\tau}}f(x)\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}p\cdot x}\,\mathrm{d}x,\qquad f(x)=\tau^{2m}\sum_{p\in(2\pi\tau\mathbb{Z})^{2m}}(\mathbf{F}_{\tau}f)(p)\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}p\cdot x}\,\mathrm{d}p.

(3.4)

Remark 3.3
.

Using periodicity of
V\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2m})
and
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2m})
one shows that the bracket
\langle V,\phi\rangle_{\tau}
does not depend on the choice of
\chi_{\tau}
.

Remark 3.4
.

Note that
G\ast\phi=\mathbf{P}_{\tau}G\ast_{\tau}\phi\in C(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})
for all
\tau\in(0,1]
,
G\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
,
\phi\in C(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})
.

Remark 3.5
.

For
\tau=0
we identify
\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2}
with
\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

Definition 3.6
.

Let
\mathbb{K}
be a finite set and let
\mathscr{T}
be a topological space. We denote by
\mathscr{T}^{\mathbb{K}}
the set of maps
\mathbb{K}\to\mathscr{T}
. We identity elements
V
of
\mathscr{T}^{\mathbb{K}}
with tuples
(V^{k})_{k\in\mathbb{K}}
and equip
\mathscr{T}^{\mathbb{K}}
with the product topology.

Definition 3.7
.

Fix
\tau\in(0,1]
and a finite set
\mathbb{K}
. For
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
U\equiv(U^{k})_{k\in\mathbb{K}^{m}}\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2m})^{\mathbb{K}^{m}}
and
\varphi\equiv(\varphi^{k})_{k\in\mathbb{K}^{m}}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2m})^{\mathbb{K}^{m}}
we write
\langle U,\varphi\rangle_{\tau}:=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{K}}\langle U^{k},\varphi^{k}\rangle_{\tau}
. For
m,n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
\varphi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2m})^{\mathbb{K}^{m}}
and
\psi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2n})^{\mathbb{K}^{n}}
we define
\varphi\otimes\psi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2(m+n)})^{\mathbb{K}^{m+n}}
by the equality

(\varphi\otimes\psi)^{(k,l)}:=\varphi^{k}\otimes\psi^{l},\qquad k\in\mathbb{K}^{m},~l\in\mathbb{K}^{n}.

(3.5)

For
G\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{\mathbb{K}^{2}}
and
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{K}}
we define
G\ast\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{K}}
by the equality

(G\ast\phi)^{k}=\sum_{l\in\mathbb{K}}G^{k,l}\ast\phi^{l}.

(3.6)

We define
\ast_{\tau}
in an analogous way.

Definition 3.8
.

Let
\tau\in(0,1]
,
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
\mathbb{K}
be a finite set. We say that
U\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2m})^{\mathbb{K}^{m}}
is antisymmetric iff

\langle U,\varphi_{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes\varphi_{m}\rangle_{\tau}=(-1)^{\mathrm{sgn}(\pi)}\langle U,\varphi_{\pi(1)}\otimes\ldots\otimes\varphi_{\pi(m)}\rangle_{\tau}

(3.7)

for all
\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{m}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{K}}
and all permutations
\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{m}
.

Definition 3.9

(Grassmann algebra)
.

Let
E
be a vector space over
\mathbb{C}
. By definition the Grassmann algebra
\mathscr{G}(E)
is the exterior algebra of
E
. We define a unique grading
\mathscr{G}=\mathscr{G}^{+}\oplus\mathscr{G}^{-}
such that
E\subset\mathscr{G}^{-}
and such that the exterior product satisfies the conditions
\mathscr{G}^{\pm}\mathscr{G}^{\pm}\subset\mathscr{G}^{+}
and
\mathscr{G}^{\pm}\mathscr{G}^{\mp}\subset\mathscr{G}^{-}
. We say that an algebra
\mathscr{A}
is a Grassmann algebra if
\mathscr{A}=\mathscr{G}(E)
for some vector space
E
. A Grassmann algebra
\mathscr{G}(E)
is finite/infinite dimensional if
E
is finite/infinite dimensional.

Remark 3.10
.

In what follows, the symbol
\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}
denotes the algebraic tensor product of vector spaces (the linear span of the set of simple tensors).

Definition 3.11
.

Let
\tau\in(0,1]
,
\mathscr{G}
be a Grassmann algebra and
\mathbb{K}
be a finite set. For
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
U\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2m})^{\mathbb{K}^{m}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}
and
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2m})^{\mathbb{K}^{m}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}
we define
\langle U,\phi\rangle_{\tau}\in\mathscr{G}
by the equality

\langle U,\phi\rangle_{\tau}:=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{l}\langle U_{i},\phi_{j}\rangle_{\tau}\,g_{i}h_{j}\in\mathscr{G},\qquad U=\sum_{i=1}^{k}U_{i}\otimes g_{i},\quad\phi=\sum_{j=1}^{l}\phi_{j}\otimes h_{j},

(3.8)

for all
k,l\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
U_{1},\ldots,U_{k}\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2m})^{\mathbb{K}^{m}}
,
g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}\in\mathscr{G}
and
\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{l}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2m})^{\mathbb{K}^{m}}
,
h_{1},\ldots,h_{l}\in\mathscr{G}
. For
m,n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
\varphi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2n})^{\mathbb{K}^{n}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}
,
\psi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2m})^{\mathbb{K}^{m}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}
we define
\varphi\otimes\psi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2(n+m)})^{\mathbb{K}^{n+m}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}
by the equality

\varphi\otimes\psi:=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{l}(\varphi_{i}\otimes\psi_{j})\otimes g_{i}h_{j},\qquad\varphi=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\varphi_{i}\otimes g_{i},\quad\psi=\sum_{j=1}^{l}\psi_{j}\otimes h_{j},

(3.9)

for all
k,l\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{k}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2n})^{\mathbb{K}^{n}}
,
g_{1},\ldots,g_{k}\in\mathscr{G}
and
\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{l}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2m})^{\mathbb{K}^{m}}
,
h_{1},\ldots,h_{l}\in\mathscr{G}
.

Remark 3.12
.

The bracket
\langle U,\phi\rangle_{\tau}
and the tensor product
\varphi\otimes\psi
do not depend on the choice of a representations of
U,\phi,\varphi,\psi
as sums of simple tensors.

Remark 3.13
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
E
be a vector space of dimension grater or equal to
m
. An antisymmetric distribution
U\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2m})^{\mathbb{K}^{m}}
is uniquely determined by the map

C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{K}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}^{-}\ni\varphi\mapsto\langle U,\varphi^{\otimes m}\rangle_{\tau}\in\mathscr{G},

(3.10)

where
\mathscr{G}:=\mathscr{G}(E)
. Indeed, given
\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{m}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{K}}
we choose
\varphi=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\psi_{j}\otimes g_{j}
, where
g_{1},\ldots,g_{m}\in\mathscr{G}
are such that
g=g_{1}\ldots g_{m}\neq 0
. Then
m!\,\langle U,\psi_{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes\psi_{m}\rangle_{\tau}\otimes g=\langle U,\varphi^{\otimes m}\rangle_{\tau}
. Note also that if
\mathscr{G}
is an infinite-dimensional Grassmann algebra, then for every
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
an antisymmetric distribution
U\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2m})^{\mathbb{K}^{m}}
is uniquely determined by the map (
3.10
).

Definition 3.14
.

Let
\tau\in(0,1]
and
\mathbb{K}
be a finite set. We call a functional a collection
U=(U^{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
such that
U^{0}\in\mathbb{C}
and
U^{m}\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2m})^{\mathbb{K}^{m}}
is antisymmetric for
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
. We denote the vector space of functionals by

\mathscr{N}(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{K}})\subset\mathbb{C}\times\bigtimes_{m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2m})^{\mathbb{K}^{m}}

(3.11)

and endow it with the product topology. Let
\mathscr{G}
be an infinite-dimensional Grassmann algebra. For
U\in\mathscr{N}(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{K}})
and
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{K}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}^{-}
we define

U(\phi):=U^{0}+\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}\langle U^{m},\phi^{\otimes m}\rangle_{\tau}\in\mathscr{G}.

(3.12)

For
U\in\mathscr{N}(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{K}})
, an entire function
f\in\mathbb{C}\to\mathbb{C}
and
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{K}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}^{-}
we define
f(U(\phi))
by

f(U(\phi)):=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}a_{n}U(\phi)^{n},\qquad f(z)=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}a_{n}\,z^{n},\quad z\in\mathbb{C},~a_{n}\in\mathbb{C},~n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}.

(3.13)

For
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
U\in\mathscr{N}(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{K}})
and
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{K}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}^{-}
the antisymmetric distribution

\mathrm{D}_{\phi}^{k}U(\phi)\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2k})^{\mathbb{K}^{k}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}

is uniquely defined by the equality

\langle\mathrm{D}_{\phi}^{k}U(\phi),\psi^{\otimes k}\rangle_{\tau}:=\partial_{u}^{k}U(\phi+u\psi)\big|_{u=0}

(3.14)

for all
\phi,\psi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{K}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}^{-}
.

Remark 3.15
.

The assumption that
\mathscr{G}
is an infinite-dimensional Grassmann algebra is only needed to ensure that for all
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
the antisymmetric distribution
\mathrm{D}_{\phi}^{k}U(\phi)
is uniquely defined, cf. Remark
3.13
. Moreover, observe that given
U\equiv(U^{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\in\mathscr{N}(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{K}})
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
it holds
\mathrm{D}_{\phi}^{k}U(\phi)|_{\phi=0}=k!\,U^{k}
.

Remark 3.16
.

Note that for all
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{K}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}^{-}
there are only finitely many non-zero terms in the series on the RHS of Eq. (
3.12
) and in the series defining
f(U(\phi))
. Indeed, if
\phi=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\phi_{i}\otimes g_{i}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{K}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}^{-}
, then
\phi^{\otimes m}=0
and
U(\phi)^{m}=0
for
m>n
. Note that the unique functional
W=(W^{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\in\mathscr{N}(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{K}})
such that
f(U(\phi))=W(\phi)
for
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{K}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}^{-}
is defined by the equations
W^{k}:=\mathrm{D}_{\phi}^{k}f(U(\phi))|_{\phi=0}
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
.

Remark 3.17
.

As an example, let us consider a functional
U\in\mathscr{N}(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2}))
of the form
U(\phi)=\phi(x_{1})\phi(x_{2})
for some fixed
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Then
\mathrm{D}_{\phi}U(\phi)=-\phi(x_{2})\delta_{x_{1}}+\phi(x_{1})\delta_{x_{2}}
and
\mathrm{D}^{2}_{\phi}U(\phi)=\delta_{x_{1}}\otimes\delta_{x_{2}}-\delta_{x_{2}}\otimes\delta_{x_{1}}
, where
\delta_{x}\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T}^{2}_{\tau})
is the periodization of the Dirac delta at
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Furthermore, we have
\langle\mathrm{D}_{\phi}U(\phi),\psi\rangle_{\tau}=\psi(x_{1})\phi(x_{2})+\phi(x_{1})\psi(x_{2})
as well as
\langle\mathrm{D}_{\phi}^{2}U(\phi),\psi^{\otimes 2}\rangle_{\tau}=2\psi(x_{1})\psi(x_{2})
.

4
Gross-Neveu model with cutoffs

In this section we introduce the Grassmann algebra of the Gross-Neveu model and use it to define the Berezin integral and the Grassmann interacting measure
\mu_{\tau,\varepsilon}
of the Gross-Neveu model with cutoffs
\tau,\varepsilon\in(0,1]
. We also state the conditions for the bump function
\vartheta_{\varepsilon}
used to implement the UV cutoff.

Definition 4.1
.

Let
\mathbb{F}:=\{1,\ldots,N\}\times\{1,2\}
,
\mathbb{G}:=\{-,+\}\times\mathbb{F}
,
\mathbb{A}:=\{0,1,2\}^{2}
. We call
\varsigma\in\mathbb{F}
and
\sigma\in\mathbb{G}
spinor indices and
a\in\mathbb{A}
a spatial multi-index.

Definition 4.2
.

The so-called gamma matrices are defined by

\gamma_{1}:=\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix},\quad\gamma_{2}:=\begin{pmatrix}0&-\mathrm{i}\\
\mathrm{i}&0\end{pmatrix},\quad\Gamma_{j}\equiv(\Gamma_{j}^{\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2}})_{\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2}\in\mathbb{F}}:=(\gamma_{j})^{\oplus N},\quad j\in\{1,2\}.

(4.1)

Remark 4.3
.

Note that
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}
are complex
2\times 2
matrices and
\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}
are complex block diagonal
2N\times 2N
matrices. It holds

\gamma_{2}^{\mathrm{t}}\gamma_{2}=-1,\qquad\gamma_{2}^{\mathrm{t}}\gamma_{1}\gamma_{2}=\gamma_{1}^{\mathrm{t}},\qquad\gamma_{2}^{\mathrm{t}}\gamma_{2}\gamma_{2}=\gamma_{2}^{\mathrm{t}},

(4.2)

where
A^{\mathrm{t}}
denotes the transposition of the matrix
A
. The matrices
\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}
satisfy the same identities.

Definition 4.4
.

For
\phi=(\phi^{\varsigma})_{\varsigma\in\mathbb{F}}\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathbb{F}}
and
\psi=(\psi^{\varsigma})_{\varsigma\in\mathbb{F}}\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathbb{F}}
we write
\phi\cdot\psi=\sum_{\varsigma\in\mathbb{F}}\phi^{\varsigma}\psi^{\varsigma}
.

Definition 4.5
.

Let
\tau,\varepsilon\in(0,1]
,
\omega(p):=(|p|^{2}+1)^{1/2}
,
\Lambda_{\tau,\varepsilon}:=\{p\in(2\pi\tau\mathbb{Z})^{2}\,|\,\varepsilon\omega(p)\leq 4\}
and
\mathcal{C}_{\tau,\varepsilon}:=(\mathrm{Span}\{x\mapsto\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}p\cdot x}\,|\,p\in\Lambda_{\tau,\varepsilon}\})^{\mathbb{G}}\subset C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}}
. The Grassmann algebra of the Gross-Neveu model
\mathscr{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon}
is the unital complex algebra whose generators

\{(\mathbf{F}_{\tau}\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}^{\sigma})(p)\,|\,p\in\Lambda_{\tau,\varepsilon}\}

(4.3)

satisfy the conditions

\displaystyle(\mathbf{F}_{\tau}\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}^{\sigma_{1}})(p_{1})\,(\mathbf{F}_{\tau}\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}^{\sigma_{2}})(p_{2})+(\mathbf{F}_{\tau}\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}^{\sigma_{2}})(p_{2})\,(\mathbf{F}_{\tau}\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}^{\sigma_{1}})(p_{1})=0

(4.4)

for all
p_{1},p_{2}\in\Lambda_{\tau,\varepsilon}
and
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in\mathbb{G}
. We define a unique grading
\mathscr{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon}=\mathscr{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon}^{+}\oplus\mathscr{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon}^{-}
such that
(\mathbf{F}_{\tau}\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}^{\sigma})(p)\in\mathscr{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon}^{-}
for all
p\in\Lambda_{\tau,\varepsilon}
,
\sigma\in\mathbb{G}
and such that the product in
\mathscr{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon}
satisfies the conditions
\mathscr{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\mathscr{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\subset\mathscr{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon}^{+}
and
\mathscr{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon}^{\pm}\mathscr{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon}^{\mp}\subset\mathscr{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon}^{-}
. Moreover, we define the free Grassmann field

\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}=(\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}^{\sigma})_{\sigma\in\mathbb{G}}\in\mathcal{C}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\otimes\mathscr{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon}^{-}\subset C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}}\otimes\mathscr{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon}^{-}

(4.5)

by the following equality

\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}^{\sigma}(x):=\tau^{2}\sum_{p\in\Lambda_{\tau,\varepsilon}}(\mathbf{F}_{\tau}\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}^{\sigma})(p)\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}p\cdot x}\,\mathrm{d}p

(4.6)

for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
\sigma\in\mathbb{G}
. For
\varsigma\in\mathbb{F}
we set
\bar{\psi}_{\tau,\varepsilon}^{\varsigma}(x):=\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}^{-,\varsigma}(x)
and
\underaccent{\bar}{\psi}_{\tau,\varepsilon}^{\varsigma}(x):=\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}^{+,\varsigma}(x)
.

Remark 4.6
.

For all
\tau,\varepsilon\in(0,1]
it holds
\mathscr{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon}=\mathscr{G}(E_{\tau,\varepsilon})
, where the vector space
E_{\tau,\varepsilon}
coincides with the span of the set (
4.3
). In particular,
\mathscr{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon}
is a finite-dimensional Grassmann algebra in the sense of Def.
3.9
.

Remark 4.7
.

Observe that for every
\tau,\varepsilon\in(0,1]
it holds
\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}^{\otimes m}=0
for all
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
provided
m>|\mathbb{G}|\,|\Lambda_{\tau,\varepsilon}|
, where
|S|
denotes the number of elements of a set
S
.

Definition 4.8
.

Let
\tau,\varepsilon\in(0,1]
. The Berezin integral is a linear map

\mathscr{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\ni g\mapsto\int g\,\mathrm{d}\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{C}

(4.7)

such that

\int g\,\mathrm{d}\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}=1,\qquad g=\prod_{p\in\Lambda_{\tau,\varepsilon}}\prod_{\varsigma\in\mathbb{F}}(\mathbf{F}_{\tau}\bar{\psi}_{\tau,\varepsilon}^{\varsigma})(p)\,(\mathbf{F}_{\tau}\underaccent{\bar}{\psi}_{\tau,\varepsilon}^{\varsigma})(p),

(4.8)

and such that it vanishes on any other monomial in the generators of
\mathscr{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon}
.

Remark 4.9
.

Note that the order in the product in Eq. (
4.8
) is unimportant because the elements of the set
\{(\mathbf{F}_{\tau}\bar{\psi}_{\tau,\varepsilon}^{\varsigma})(p)\,(\mathbf{F}_{\tau}\underaccent{\bar}{\psi}_{\tau,\varepsilon}^{\varsigma})(p)\,|\,p\in\Lambda_{\tau,\varepsilon},\,\varsigma\in\mathbb{F}\}
commute. For a discussion of properties of the Berezin integral see for example
[
CSS13
, Appendix A]
.

Remark 4.10
.

Let
\mathscr{G}_{1},\mathscr{G}_{2}
be Grassmann algebras. We denote by
\mathscr{G}_{1}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}_{2}
the algebraic graded tensor product of graded algebras
\mathscr{G}_{1}
and
\mathscr{G}_{2}
. Note that
\mathscr{G}_{1}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}_{2}
is a Grassmann algebra. Given
g_{1}\in\mathscr{G}_{1}
and
g_{2}\in\mathscr{G}_{2}
we define
g_{1}+g_{2}:=g_{1}\otimes 1+1\otimes g_{2}\in\mathscr{G}_{1}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}_{2}
.

Remark 4.11
.

Let
\mathscr{G}
be a Grassmann algebra. We define the Berezin integral on
\mathscr{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}
as a linear map

\mathscr{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}\ni g\mapsto\int g\,\mathrm{d}\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}\in\mathscr{G}

(4.9)

such that

\int(g\otimes h)\,\mathrm{d}\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}:=\left(\int g\,\mathrm{d}\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}\right)h

(4.10)

for all
g\in\mathscr{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon}
and
h\in\mathscr{G}
.

Lemma 4.12
.

Let
\tau,\varepsilon\in(0,1]
and
\mathscr{G}
be a Grassmann algebra. For all
F\in\mathscr{N}(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{K}})
and
\phi\in\mathcal{C}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\otimes\mathscr{G}^{-}\subset C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}}\otimes\mathscr{G}^{-}
it holds

\int F(\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}+\phi)\,\mathrm{d}\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}=\int F(\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon})\,\mathrm{d}\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon},

(4.11)

where
\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}+\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}}\otimes(\mathscr{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\otimes\mathscr{G})^{-}
.

Proof.

The lemma follows from the invariance under translation of the Berezin integral, cf.
[
CSS13
, Proposition A.12]
.
∎

Definition 4.13
.

Let
\zeta_{\flat}\in(4/5,1)
and
\vartheta\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
be a function belonging to the Gevrey class of order
1/\zeta_{\flat}
such that
\vartheta(t)=1
for
t\in(-\infty,1/2]
and
\vartheta(t)=0
for
t\in[1,\infty)
. For
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
we define
\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
by
\mathbf{F}\vartheta_{\varepsilon}(p)=\vartheta(2\varepsilon\omega(p))^{1/2}
.

Remark 4.14
.

Recall that the class of Gevrey functions of order
s\in[1,\infty)
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
consists of
\varphi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that for some
C\in(0,\infty)
the bound

\sup_{p\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\partial^{a}\varphi(p)|\leq C^{1+|a|}\,(a!)^{s}

(4.12)

is satisfied for all
a\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
.

Remark 4.15
.

For an explicit construction of a function
\vartheta
satisfying the conditions stated in the above definition see
[
GMR21
, App. A.1]
.

Definition 4.16
.

The free action functional
A_{\tau}\in\mathscr{N}(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}})
and the interaction term
U_{\tau,\varepsilon}\equiv U_{\tau}(g_{\tau,\varepsilon},r_{\tau,\varepsilon})\in\mathscr{N}(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}})
with cutoffs
\tau,\varepsilon\in(0,1]
are defined uniquely by Eqs. (
1.3
) and (
1.4
)
for all
\psi\equiv(\bar{\psi},\underaccent{\bar}{\psi})\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}^{-}\equiv(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{F}}\times C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{F}})\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}^{-}
, where
\mathscr{G}
is an infinite-dimensional Grassmann algebra. The free and interacting measures with cutoffs
\tau,\varepsilon\in(0,1]
are defined with the use of the Berezin integral introduced in Def.
4.8
by the equations

\nu_{\tau,\varepsilon}(F):=\frac{\int F(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\ast\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon})\exp(-A_{\tau}(\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon})))\,\mathrm{d}\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}}{\int\exp(-A_{\tau}(\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}))\,\mathrm{d}\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}}

(4.13)

and

\mu_{\tau,\varepsilon}(F):=\frac{\int F(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\ast\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon})\exp(-A_{\tau}(\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon})+U_{\tau,\varepsilon}(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\ast\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}))\,\mathrm{d}\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}}{\int\exp(-A_{\tau}(\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon})+U_{\tau,\varepsilon}(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\ast\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}))\,\mathrm{d}\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}},

(4.14)

respectively, for all functionals
F\in\mathscr{N}(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}})
, where
\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}}\otimes\mathscr{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon}^{-}
is defined by Eq. (
4.6
).

Remark 4.17
.

Note that by
[
CSS13
, Proposition A.14]
it holds

\int\exp(-A_{\tau}(\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}))\,\mathrm{d}\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}=\prod_{p\in\Lambda_{\tau,\varepsilon}}\det((-\Xi^{\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2}}(p))_{\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2}\in\mathbb{F}})=\prod_{p\in\Lambda_{\tau,\varepsilon}}(-1)^{N}\,(1+|p|^{2})^{N}\neq 0,

(4.15)

where
\Xi^{\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2}}(p):=1^{\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2}}-\sum_{j\in\{1,2\}}\mathrm{i}\Gamma_{j}^{\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2}}p^{j}
for
\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2}\in\mathbb{F}
. As a result, the free field measure is well-defined for all
\tau,\varepsilon\in(0,1]
. The interacting measure with cutoffs
\tau,\varepsilon\in(0,1]
is well-defined provided

\frac{\int\exp(-A_{\tau}(\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon})+U_{\tau,\varepsilon}(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\ast\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}))\,\mathrm{d}\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}}{\int\exp(-A_{\tau}(\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}))\,\mathrm{d}\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}}=\nu_{\tau,\varepsilon}(\exp(U_{\tau,\varepsilon}({\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}})))\neq 0.

(4.16)

This is proved in Remark
14.4
for a specific choice of parameters
g_{\tau,\varepsilon}
,
r_{\tau,\varepsilon}
fixed as in Theorem
1.1
.

5
Scale decomposition of propagator

In this section we define the scale decomposition
G_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}
of the propagator
G_{\tau,\varepsilon}
. We also introduce auxiliary propagators
G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\varepsilon;t}
that will be used in Sec.
6
to construct the scale decomposition
\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}
of the free field that satisfies the conditions discussed in Sec.
2
.

Definition 5.1
.

For
\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2}\in\mathbb{F}
and
p\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
let
\Xi^{\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2}}(p):=1^{\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2}}-\sum_{j\in\{1,2\}}\mathrm{i}\Gamma_{j}^{\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2}}p^{j}
and
\omega(p):=(|p|^{2}+1)^{1/2}
. The free field covariance

G\equiv(G^{\sigma})_{\sigma\in\mathbb{G}^{2}}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}^{2}}

is defined by
