G^{\sigma}(x)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\mathbf{F}G^{\sigma})(p)\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}p\cdot x}\,\mathrm{d}p,

(5.1)

where

\begin{gathered}(\mathbf{F}G^{+,\varsigma_{1},+,\varsigma_{2}})(p):=0,\qquad(\mathbf{F}G^{+,\varsigma_{1},-,\varsigma_{2}})(p):=\Xi^{\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2}}(p)/(\omega(p))^{2},\\
(\mathbf{F}G^{-,\varsigma_{1},-,\varsigma_{2}})(p):=0,\qquad(\mathbf{F}G^{-,\varsigma_{1},+,\varsigma_{2}})(p):=-(\mathbf{F}G^{+,\varsigma_{2},-,\varsigma_{1}})(-p)\end{gathered}

(5.2)

for all
\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2}\in\mathbb{F}
. For
\varepsilon,t\in[0,1]
we define

G_{\varepsilon}\equiv(G^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon})_{\sigma\in\mathbb{G}^{2}},~G_{\varepsilon;t}\equiv(G^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon;t})_{\sigma\in\mathbb{G}^{2}},~\dot{G}_{\varepsilon;t}\equiv(\dot{G}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon;t})_{\sigma\in\mathbb{G}^{2}}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}^{2}},

(5.3)

by the equalities

(\mathbf{F}G^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon})(p):=\vartheta(2\varepsilon\omega(p))\,(\mathbf{F}G^{\sigma})(p),\quad(\mathbf{F}G^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon;t})(p):=\vartheta(t\omega(p))\,(\mathbf{F}G^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon})(p),\quad\dot{G}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon;t}:=\partial_{t}G^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon;t},

(5.4)

where the bump function
\vartheta\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
was introduced in Def.
4.13
. For
\varepsilon\in[0,1]
,
t\in(0,1]
and
\sigma\in\mathbb{G}^{2}
,
\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}\in\mathbb{F}
we define

G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\varepsilon;t}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2}),

by the equalities

(\mathbf{F}G^{+;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\varepsilon;t})(p):=-(\mathbf{F}\dot{G}^{+,\varsigma,-,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\varepsilon;t})(p),\qquad(\mathbf{F}G^{-;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\varepsilon;t})(p):=\vartheta(\varepsilon/2t)\,\vartheta(t\omega(p)/2)\,1^{\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}.

(5.5)

For
\tau\in(0,1]
the periodizations of the kernels
G^{\sigma},G^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon},G^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon;t},\dot{G}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon;t},G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\varepsilon;t}
are defined with the use of the operator
\mathbf{P}_{\tau}
introduced in Def.
3.2
and are denoted by
G^{\sigma}_{\tau},G^{\sigma}_{\tau,\varepsilon},G^{\sigma}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t},\dot{G}^{\sigma}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t},G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}
, respectively. We use the symbols
\dot{G}^{\sigma}_{t},G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{t}
to denote
\dot{G}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon;t},G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\varepsilon;t}
with
\varepsilon=0
.

Remark 5.2
.

The kernels introduced in the above definition have the following properties.

(A)

If
t\in(0,\varepsilon]
, then
\vartheta(2\varepsilon\omega(p))\vartheta(t\omega(p))=\vartheta(2\varepsilon\omega(p))
and
G^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon;t}=G^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon}
,
\dot{G}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon;t}=0
.

(B)

If
t\in(0,\varepsilon/2]
, then
G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\varepsilon;t}=0
. Moreover,
\mathrm{supp}\,\mathbf{F}_{\tau}G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}\subset\Lambda_{\tau,\varepsilon}
for all
t\in(0,1]
.

(C)

If
t\in[4\varepsilon,1]
, then
\vartheta(2\varepsilon\omega(p))\dot{\vartheta}(t\omega(p))=\dot{\vartheta}(t\omega(p))
and
\dot{G}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon;t}=\dot{G}^{\sigma}_{t}
,
G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\varepsilon;t}=G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{t}
.

(D)

If
t\in(0,1/2)
, then
\dot{\vartheta}(t\omega(0))=0
and
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\dot{G}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon;t}=0
.

(E)

Since
\vartheta(\varepsilon/2t)\,\vartheta(t\omega(p)/2)=1
on the support of
p\mapsto\vartheta(2\varepsilon\omega(p))\,\dot{\vartheta}(t\omega(p))
, it holds

\dot{G}^{+;\varsigma,-,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\varepsilon;t}=-\textstyle\sum_{\hat{\varsigma}\in\mathbb{F}}G^{+;\varsigma,\hat{\varsigma}}_{\varepsilon;t}\ast G^{-;\hat{\varsigma},\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\varepsilon;t},\qquad\dot{G}^{+;\varsigma,-,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}=-\textstyle\sum_{\hat{\varsigma}\in\mathbb{F}}G^{+;\varsigma,\hat{\varsigma}}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}\ast_{\tau}G^{-;\hat{\varsigma},\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}.

(5.6)

Note also that
\dot{G}^{+,\varsigma,-,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\varepsilon;t}(x)=-\dot{G}^{-,\tilde{\varsigma},+,\varsigma}_{\varepsilon;t}(-x)
as well as
\dot{G}^{+,\varsigma;+,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\varepsilon;t}=0
and
\dot{G}^{-,\varsigma;-,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\varepsilon;t}=0
.

(F)

Since
G_{\varepsilon;1}=0
it holds
G_{\varepsilon}=-\int_{\varepsilon}^{1}\dot{G}_{\varepsilon;t}\,\mathrm{d}t
and
G-G_{\varepsilon}=-\int_{0}^{4\varepsilon}(\dot{G}_{t}-\dot{G}_{\varepsilon;t})\,\mathrm{d}t
.

Lemma 5.3
.

For all
a\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{2}
there exist
\delta\in(0,1)
and
C\in(0,\infty)
such that for all
\varepsilon\in[0,1]
,
t\in(0,1]
,
\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2}\in\mathbb{F}
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
it holds

\exp(\delta\,t^{-\zeta_{\flat}}\,|x|^{\zeta_{\flat}})~|\partial^{a}G_{\varepsilon;t}^{\pm;\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2}}(x)|\leq C\,t^{-|a|-2},

(5.7)

where
\zeta_{\flat}\in(4/5,1)
is the parameter introduced in Def.
4.13
.

Proof.

We first note the following identities

x^{b}\partial^{a}G_{\varepsilon;t}^{+,\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2}}(x)=\frac{\mathrm{i}^{|a|-|b|}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\partial^{b}_{p}\Big(p^{a}\,\vartheta(2\varepsilon\omega(p))\,\dot{\vartheta}(t\omega(p))\,\Xi^{\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2}}(p)/\omega(p)\Big)\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}p\cdot x}\,\mathrm{d}p

(5.8)

and

x^{b}\partial^{a}G_{\varepsilon;t}^{-,\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2}}(x)=\frac{\mathrm{i}^{|a|-|b|}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\partial^{b}_{p}\Big(p^{a}\,\vartheta(\varepsilon/2t)\,\vartheta(t\omega(p)/2)\,1^{\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2}}\Big)\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}p\cdot x}\,\mathrm{d}p.

(5.9)

Note that the composition of a Gevrey function of order
s\in[1,\infty)
with an analytic function is a Gevrey function of order
s\in[1,\infty)
, cf.
[
Rod93
, Prop. 1.4.6]
. Consequently, since the function
\vartheta
is flat in the vicinity of the origin the function

\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\ni(\varepsilon,p)\mapsto\vartheta((\varepsilon^{2}+p^{2})^{1/2})\in\mathbb{R}

(5.10)

belongs to the Gevrey class of order
1/{\zeta_{\flat}}
. As a result, there exists
C\in(0,\infty)
such that for all
\varepsilon\in[0,1]
and
a\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{2}
it holds

\sup_{p\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\partial^{a}_{p}\vartheta(\varepsilon\omega(p))|=\varepsilon^{|a|}\sup_{p\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\partial^{a}_{p}\vartheta((\varepsilon^{2}+p^{2})^{1/2})|\leq C^{1+|a|}\,(a!)^{1/{\zeta_{\flat}}}\,\varepsilon^{|a|}.

(5.11)

By a similar argument, there exists
C\in(0,\infty)
such that for all
t\in[0,1]
and
a\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{2}
it holds

\displaystyle\sup_{p\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\partial^{a}_{p}\vartheta(t\omega(p)/2)|

\displaystyle=t^{|a|}\sup_{p\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\partial^{a}_{p}\vartheta((t^{2}+p^{2})^{1/2}/2)|\leq C^{1+|a|}\,(a!)^{1/{\zeta_{\flat}}}\,t^{|a|},

(5.12)

\displaystyle\sup_{p\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\partial^{a}_{p}\dot{\vartheta}(t\omega(p))|

\displaystyle=t^{|a|}\sup_{p\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\partial^{a}_{p}\dot{\vartheta}((t^{2}+p^{2})^{1/2})|\leq C^{1+|a|}\,(a!)^{1/{\zeta_{\flat}}}\,t^{|a|}.

Since the function
p\mapsto\Xi^{\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2}}(p)/\omega(p)\in\mathbb{R}
is analytic in a strip of size one around the real axis there exists
C\in(0,\infty)
such that for all
a\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{2}
it holds

\sup_{p\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\partial^{a}_{p}(\Xi^{\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2}}(p)/\omega(p))|\leq C^{1+|a|}\,a!\,.

(5.13)

Noting that
\vartheta(2\varepsilon\omega(p))\,\dot{\vartheta}(t\omega(p))
vanishes unless
t\omega(p)\in(1/2,1)
and
t>\varepsilon
and
\vartheta(t\omega(p)/2)
vanishes unless
t\omega(p)\in[0,2]
and using the general Leibniz rule we show there exists
C\in(0,\infty)
such that for all
\varepsilon\in[0,1]
,
t\in(0,1]
it holds

\partial^{b}_{p}\left(p^{a}\,\vartheta(2\varepsilon\omega(p))\,\dot{\vartheta}(t\omega(p))\,\Xi^{\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2}}(p)/\omega(p)\right)\leq C^{1+|b|}\,(b!)^{1/{\zeta_{\flat}}}\,t^{|b|-|a|}\,1_{[0,2]}(t\omega(p))

(5.14)

and

\partial^{b}_{p}\left(p^{a}\,\vartheta(\varepsilon/2t)\,\vartheta(t\omega(p)/2)\,1^{\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2}}\right)\leq C^{1+|b|}\,(b!)^{1/{\zeta_{\flat}}}\,t^{|b|-|a|}\,1_{[0,2]}(t\omega(p)).

(5.15)

Using Eqs. (
5.8
) and (
5.9
) we obtain that there exists
C\in(0,\infty)
such that for all
\varepsilon\in[0,1]
,
t\in(0,1]
and
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
it holds

(|x|/t)^{k}~|\partial^{a}G_{\varepsilon;t}^{\pm,\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2}}(x)|\leq C^{1+k}\,(k!)^{1/{\zeta_{\flat}}}\,t^{-|a|-2}/2

(5.16)

We conclude that there exists
C\in[\mathrm{e},\infty)
such that for all
\varepsilon\in[0,1]
,
t\in(0,1]
and
l\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
it holds

(|x|/t)^{{\zeta_{\flat}}l}~|\partial^{a}G_{\varepsilon;t}^{\pm,\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2}}(x)|\leq C^{1+k}\,(k!)^{1/{\zeta_{\flat}}}\,t^{-|a|-2}/2\leq C^{1+3l/{\zeta_{\flat}}}\,l!\,t^{-|a|-2}/2

(5.17)

where
k=\lceil{\zeta_{\flat}}l\rceil\leq l\wedge({\zeta_{\flat}}l+1)
. The second of the above bounds follows from the estimates
(k!)^{1/{\zeta_{\flat}}}\leq k^{k/{\zeta_{\flat}}}\leq l^{l+1/{\zeta_{\flat}}}\leq\mathrm{e}^{2l/{\zeta_{\flat}}}l!
. This implies that the bound stated in the lemma holds true for
\delta=C^{-3/{\zeta_{\flat}}}/2
.
∎

6
Scale decomposition of free field

In this section we introduce the scale decomposition of the free field
\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}
satisfying properties listed in Sec.
2
. The scale decomposition of the free field is defined in the non-commutative probability space
(\mathscr{F},\mathbf{E})
of the fermionic spacetime white noise
\xi
. We also introduce a filtration
(\mathscr{F}_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}
in
\mathscr{F}
as well as the conditional expected value
\mathbf{E}_{t}
that will play an essential role in the proof of the estimates stated in Sec.
12
.

Definition 6.1
.

Let
\mathfrak{h}
be a Hilbert space. We denote by
\Gamma_{\mathrm{a}}(\mathfrak{h})
the antisymmetric Fock space built on
\mathfrak{h}
. We denote by
\mathds{N}
the number operator and by
\mathds{P}:=(-1)^{\mathds{N}}
the parity operator.

Definition 6.2
.

Let

\mathscr{H}=\Gamma_{\mathrm{a}}(L^{2}([0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{F}))^{\otimes 2}.

The creation and annihilation operators in
\Gamma_{\mathrm{a}}(L^{2}([0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{F}))
are denoted by
a^{*}(f,\varsigma)
and
a(f,\varsigma)
, where
f\in L^{2}([0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
and
\varsigma\in\mathbb{F}
.
The vacuum state in
\mathscr{H}
is denoted by
\Omega
. Let
\mathscr{B}:=\mathscr{B}(\mathscr{H})
be the Banach algebra of bounded operators on
\mathscr{H}
. The fermionic white noise is defined by

\displaystyle\xi^{-,\varsigma}(f)\equiv\int_{[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}f(s,x)\,\xi^{-,\varsigma}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}x):=(\mathds{P}\otimes a^{*}(f,\varsigma)-a(f^{\mathrm{c}},\varsigma)\otimes\mathds{1}),

(6.1)

\displaystyle\xi^{+,\varsigma}(f)\equiv\int_{[0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}f(s,x)\,\xi^{+,\varsigma}(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}x):=(a^{*}(f,\varsigma)\otimes\mathds{1}+\mathds{P}\otimes a(f^{\mathrm{c}},\varsigma))

(6.2)

for all
f\in L^{2}([0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
,
\varsigma\in\mathbb{F}
, where
f^{\mathrm{c}}
denotes the complex conjugate of the function
f
. We define
\mathscr{F}
to be the unital Banach subalgebra of
\mathscr{B}
generated by
\xi^{\pm,\varsigma}(f)
, where
f\in L^{2}([0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
,
\varsigma\in\mathbb{F}
. We define a unique grading
\mathscr{F}=\mathscr{F}^{+}\oplus\mathscr{F}^{-}
such that
\xi^{\pm,\varsigma}(f)\in\mathscr{F}^{-}
and such that the product in
\mathscr{F}
satisfies the conditions
\mathscr{F}^{\pm}\mathscr{F}^{\pm}\subset\mathscr{F}^{+}
and
\mathscr{F}^{\pm}\mathscr{F}^{\mp}\subset\mathscr{F}^{-}
. For
s\in[0,1]
and
t\in[s,1]
we define
\mathscr{F}_{s,t}
to be the unital Banach subalgebra of
\mathscr{F}
generated by
\xi^{\pm,\varsigma}(f)
, where
f\in L^{2}([0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
,
\varsigma\in\mathbb{F}
,
\mathrm{supp}\,f\subset[s,t]\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
. For
t\in[0,1]
we we set
\mathscr{F}_{t}:=\mathscr{F}_{1,t}
.

Remark 6.3
.

We note that that
\mathscr{F}
is an infinite-dimensional Grassmann algebra. One shows that elements of
\mathscr{F}^{-}
anti-commute with each other and elements of
\mathscr{F}^{+}
commute with all elements of
\mathscr{F}
.

Remark 6.4
.

It what follows, the following bound

\textstyle\sum_{\varsigma\in\mathbb{F}}\|f^{\varsigma}\|_{L^{2}([0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\leq\left\|\textstyle\sum_{\varsigma\in\mathbb{F}}\xi^{\pm,\varsigma}(f^{\varsigma})\right\|_{\mathscr{B}}^{2}\leq 2\textstyle\sum_{\varsigma\in\mathbb{F}}\|f^{\varsigma}\|_{L^{2}([0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}

(6.3)

valid for all
f=(f^{\varsigma})_{\varsigma\in\mathbb{F}}\in L^{2}([0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{2})^{\mathbb{F}}
will play an important role. To prove the first bound we apply the operator to the vacuum state
\Omega
. The second of the above bounds is an immediate consequence of the identity

\|\textstyle\sum_{\varsigma\in\mathbb{F}}a(f^{\varsigma},\varsigma)\|_{\mathscr{B}}=\|\textstyle\sum_{\varsigma\in\mathbb{F}}a^{*}(f^{\varsigma},\varsigma)\|_{\mathscr{B}}=\textstyle\sum_{\varsigma\in\mathbb{F}}\|f^{\varsigma}\|_{L^{2}([0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{2})},

(6.4)

which follows easily from the anti-commutation relations of the fermionic creation and annihilation operators, for details see for example
[
BR97
, Proposition 5.2.2]
.

Definition 6.5

(Expected value)
.

We define a continuous linear functional
\mathbf{E}\,:\,\mathscr{F}\to\mathbb{C}
by the equality
\mathbf{E}F:=(\Omega,F\Omega)
for all
F\in\mathscr{F}
.

Remark 6.6
.

Note that it holds

\mathbf{E}(\xi^{\pm,\varsigma}(f)\,\xi^{\mp,\tilde{\varsigma}}(\tilde{f}))=\pm 1_{\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}\,\langle f,\tilde{f}\rangle_{L^{2}([0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{2})},\qquad\mathbf{E}(\xi^{\pm,\varsigma}(f)\,\xi^{\pm,\tilde{\varsigma}}(\tilde{f}))=0

(6.5)

for all
f,\tilde{f}\in L^{2}([0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
,
\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}\in\mathbb{F}
.

Definition 6.7

(Conditional expected value)
.

A family of continuous linear functionals
(\mathbf{E}_{t}\,:\,\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{F})_{t\in[0,1]}
is called the conditional expected value if

(0)

\mathbf{E}_{t}\,:\,\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{F}_{t}
for all
t\in[0,1]
,

(1)

\mathbf{E}_{t}F=\mathbf{E}F
for all
t\in[0,1]
and
F\in\mathscr{F}_{0,t}
,

(2)

\mathbf{E}_{t}=\mathbf{E}_{t}\mathbf{E}_{s}:\,\mathscr{F}\to\mathscr{F}_{t}
for
s\in[0,1]
and
t\in[s,1]
,

(3)

\mathbf{E}_{t}(GFH)=G\mathbf{E}_{t}(F)H
for all
t\in[0,1]
and
G,H\in\mathscr{F}_{t}
,
F\in\mathscr{F}
,

(4)

\|\mathbf{E}_{t}F\|_{\mathscr{B}}\leq\|F\|_{\mathscr{B}}
for all
t\in[0,1]
and
F\in\mathscr{F}
.

Remark 6.8
.

For the construction of the conditional expected value see
[
DFG22
, Appendix A]
.

Remark 6.9
.

Note that the condition (1) implies in particular that
\mathbf{E}_{1}=\mathbf{E}
.

Remark 6.10
.

Let
\mathscr{G}
be a Grassmann algebra. We define
\mathscr{F}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}
to be the algebraic graded tensor product of graded algebras
\mathscr{F}
and
\mathscr{G}
. We introduce the family of functionals
(\mathbf{E}_{t}\,:\,\mathscr{F}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}\to\mathscr{F}_{t}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G})_{t\in[0,1]}
defined uniquely by the equality
\mathbf{E}_{t}(F\otimes g)=(\mathbf{E}_{t}F)\otimes g
for all
F\in\mathscr{F}
,
g\in\mathscr{G}
and
t\in[0,1]
. The functional
\mathbf{E}\,:\,\mathscr{F}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}\to\mathscr{G}
is defined in an analogous way.

Definition 6.11
.

Let
\tau,\varepsilon\in[0,1]
,
s\in[0,1]
and
t\in[s,1]
. We define operator-valued distributions

\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}\equiv(\varPsi^{-}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s},\varPsi^{+}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s})\equiv(\bar{\varPsi}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s},\underaccent{\bar}{\varPsi}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s})\equiv(\varPsi^{\sigma}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s})_{\sigma\in\mathbb{G}}\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2},\mathscr{F}_{t,s}\cap\mathscr{F}^{-})^{\mathbb{G}}

(6.6)

by the equation

\langle\varPsi^{\pm,\varsigma}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s},\varphi\rangle:=\sum_{\tilde{\varsigma}\in\mathbb{F}}\int_{[s,t]\times\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2}}(G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u}\ast\varphi)(x)\,\xi^{\pm,\tilde{\varsigma}}(\mathrm{d}u,\mathrm{d}x)

(6.7)

for all
\varsigma\in\mathbb{F}
and
\varphi\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
. We call
\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon}:=\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;1,0}
the free field with IR cutoff
\tau\in[0,1]
and UV cutoff
\varepsilon\in[0,1]
. We also define
\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;s}:=\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;1,s}
. We omit
\tau
and
\varepsilon
if
\tau=0
and
\varepsilon=0
.

Remark 6.12
.

Observe that by Remark
5.2
(B) for
\tau,\varepsilon\in(0,1]
it holds

\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon}^{\sigma}(x):=\tau^{2}\sum_{p\in\Lambda_{\tau,\varepsilon}}(\mathbf{F}_{\tau}\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon}^{\sigma})(p)\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}p\cdot x}.

(6.8)

In particular,
\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon}^{\otimes m}=0
for all
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
provided
m>|\mathbb{G}|\,|\Lambda_{\tau,\varepsilon}|
, where
|S|
denotes the number of elements of a set
S
.

Remark 6.13
.

Let
\mathscr{G}
be a Grassmann algebra. For
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}
we define the paring
\langle\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s},\phi\rangle_{\tau}\in\mathscr{F}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}
by the equality

\langle\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s},\phi\rangle_{\tau}:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s},\phi_{i}\rangle_{\tau}\otimes g_{i},\qquad\phi=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\phi_{i}\otimes g_{i},

(6.9)

for all
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
\phi_{1},\ldots,\phi_{n}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}}
and
g_{1},\ldots,g_{n}\in\mathscr{G}
. We identify
A\in\mathscr{F}
and
g\in\mathscr{G}
with

A\otimes 1\in\mathscr{F}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}\qquad\mathrm{and}\qquad\mathds{1}\otimes g\in\mathscr{F}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G},

(6.10)

respectively, and use these identifications to make sense of
A+g,Ag\in\mathscr{F}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}
.

Remark 6.14
.

Let
\tau,\varepsilon\in[0,1]
,
s\in[0,1]
and
t\in[s,1]
be such that
\varepsilon>0
or
s>0
. Using Remark
6.4
, the fact that
G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\varepsilon;t}=0
for
t\in(0,\varepsilon/2]
by Remark
5.2
(B) and Lemma
5.3
one shows that
\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2},\mathscr{F}_{t,s})^{\mathbb{G}}
. Note that it holds
\mathbf{E}\varPsi^{\sigma}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}(x)=0
and

\mathbf{E}(\varPsi^{\sigma_{1}}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}(x)\,\varPsi^{\sigma_{2}}_{\tau,\varepsilon;s}(y))=G^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}(x-y),

(6.11)

where
G_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}^{2}}
was introduced in Def.
5.1
. Using the equality
\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}=\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;s}-\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}
one shows that it holds
\mathbf{E}\varPsi^{\sigma}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}(x)=0
and

\mathbf{E}(\varPsi^{\sigma_{1}}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}(x)\,\varPsi^{\sigma_{2}}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}(y))=\mathbf{E}(\,(\varPsi^{\sigma_{1}}_{\tau,\varepsilon;s}(x)-\varPsi^{\sigma_{1}}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}(x))\,(\varPsi^{\sigma_{2}}_{\tau,\varepsilon;s}(y)-\varPsi^{\sigma_{2}}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}(y))\,)\\
=(G_{\tau,\varepsilon;s}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}-G_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}})(x-y).

(6.12)

The expected values of products of more than two fields
\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}
can be expressed in terms of the covariance with the use of the generating functional

C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}^{-}\ni\phi\mapsto\mathbf{E}\exp(\langle\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s},\phi\rangle_{\tau})=\exp(-\langle\phi,(G_{\varepsilon;s}-G_{\varepsilon;t})\ast\phi\rangle_{\tau}/2)\in\mathscr{G},

(6.13)

where
\mathscr{G}
is an infinite-dimensional Grassmann algebra. In order to prove (
6.13
) one decomposes
\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}
into a sum of two terms
\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}^{(\pm)}
involving only the creation and annihilation operators, applies the Baker–Campbell–Hausdorff formula

\exp(\langle\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s},\phi\rangle_{\tau})=\exp(\langle\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}^{(+)},\phi\rangle_{\tau})\,\exp(\langle\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}^{(-)},\phi\rangle_{\tau})\,\exp(-[\langle\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}^{(+)},\phi\rangle_{\tau},\langle\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}^{(-)},\phi\rangle_{\tau}]/2),

(6.14)

where
[{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}},{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}]
denotes the commutator, and uses the fact that the vacuum state
\Omega
belongs to the intersection of the kernels of the annihilation operators.

Lemma 6.15
.

Let
\tau,\varepsilon\in(0,1]
. For all
F\in\mathscr{N}(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}})
it holds

\nu_{\tau,\varepsilon}(F)\equiv\frac{\int F(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\ast\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon})\exp(-A_{\tau}(\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}))\,\mathrm{d}\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}}{\int\exp(-A_{\tau}(\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}))\,\mathrm{d}\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}}=\mathbf{E}F(\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon}).

(6.15)

Remark 6.16
.

Note that in particular it holds

\int\psi^{\sigma_{1}}(x)\psi^{\sigma_{2}}(y)\,\nu_{\tau,\varepsilon}(\mathrm{d}\psi)=G_{\tau,\varepsilon}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}(x-y).

(6.16)

Proof.

In view of Remarks
4.7
and
6.12
it suffices to prove the stated equality for functionals
F=(F^{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\in\mathscr{N}(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}})
such that
F^{m}\neq 0
only for finitely many
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. In consequence, it is enough to prove the stated equality for functionals
F=F_{\eta}
for all
\eta\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}^{-}
, where the functional
F_{\eta}\in\mathscr{N}(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}})
is defined by the equality
F_{\eta}(\phi):=\exp(\langle\phi,\eta\rangle_{\tau})
for all
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}^{-}
. By
[
CSS13
, Theorem A.16]
it holds

\frac{\int F_{\eta}(\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon})\exp(-A_{\tau}(\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}))\,\mathrm{d}\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}}{\int\exp(-A_{\tau}(\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}))\,\mathrm{d}\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}}=\exp(-\langle\eta,\tilde{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\ast_{\tau}\eta\rangle_{\tau}/2),

(6.17)

where
\mathbf{F}_{\tau}\tilde{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon}(p):=1_{\Lambda_{\tau,\varepsilon}}(p)\,\mathbf{F}G(p)
. Note that

F_{\eta}(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\ast\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon})=F_{\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\ast\eta}(\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}).

(6.18)

Using the fact that
\mathrm{supp}\,\mathbf{F}_{\tau}\mathbf{P}_{\tau}\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\subset\Lambda_{\tau,\varepsilon}
we show that

\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\ast\tilde{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\ast\vartheta_{\varepsilon}=\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\ast G_{\tau}\ast\vartheta_{\varepsilon}=G_{\tau,\varepsilon}

(6.19)

and

\frac{\int F_{\eta}(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\ast\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon})\exp(-A_{\tau}(\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}))\,\mathrm{d}\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}}{\int\exp(-A_{\tau}(\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}))\,\mathrm{d}\psi_{\tau,\varepsilon}}=\exp(-\langle\eta,G_{\tau,\varepsilon}\ast_{\tau}\eta\rangle_{\tau}/2).

(6.20)

On the other hand,

\mathbf{E}F_{\eta}(\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon})=\exp(-\langle\eta,G_{\tau,\varepsilon}\ast_{\tau}\eta\rangle_{\tau}/2)

(6.21)

by Eq. (
6.13
) and Remark
3.4
. This finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma 6.17
.

Let
\tau,\varepsilon\in(0,1]
,
s\in[0,1]
and
t\in[s,1]
. For all
F\in\mathscr{N}(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}})
and
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}^{-}
it holds

\mathbf{E}F(\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}+\phi)=(\exp(\mathbf{D}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}/2)F)(\phi),

(6.22)

where the map
\mathbf{D}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}
is defined by the equation

(\mathbf{D}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}F)(\phi):=\langle\mathrm{D}_{\phi}^{2}F(\phi),(G_{\tau,\varepsilon;s}-G_{\tau,\varepsilon;t})({\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}-{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}})\rangle_{\tau}

(6.23)

for all
F\in\mathscr{N}(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}})
and
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}^{-}
.

Proof.

By the argument from the proof of Lemma
6.15
it suffices to prove the statement for all functionals of the form
F=F_{\eta}
for some
\eta\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}^{-}
, where
F_{\eta}(\phi):=\exp(\langle\phi,\eta\rangle_{\tau})
for all
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}^{-}
. By direct computation we obtain

\mathbf{D}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}F_{\eta}=\langle\eta,(G_{\tau,\varepsilon;s}-G_{\tau,\varepsilon;t})\ast_{\tau}\eta\rangle_{\tau}~F_{\eta}.

(6.24)

Since
\langle\eta,(G_{\tau,\varepsilon;s}-G_{\tau,\varepsilon;t})\ast_{\tau}\eta\rangle_{\tau}\in\mathscr{G}^{+}
we arrive at

\exp(\mathbf{D}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}/2)F_{\eta}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{n}n!}\mathbf{D}^{n}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}F_{\eta}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{n}n!}\langle\eta,(G_{\tau,\varepsilon;s}-G_{\tau,\varepsilon;t})\ast_{\tau}\eta\rangle_{\tau}^{n}~F_{\eta}\\
=\exp(-\langle\eta,(G_{\tau,\varepsilon;s}-G_{\tau,\varepsilon;t})\ast_{\tau}\eta\rangle_{\tau}/2)~F_{\eta}.

(6.25)

On the other hand,

\mathbf{E}F_{\eta}(\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}+\phi)=\mathbf{E}F_{\eta}(\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s})~F_{\eta}(\phi)=\exp(-\langle\eta,(G_{\tau,\varepsilon;s}-G_{\tau,\varepsilon;t})\ast_{\tau}\eta\rangle_{\tau}/2)~F_{\eta}(\phi)

(6.26)

by Eq. (
6.13
) and Remark
3.4
. This finishes the proof.
∎

Definition 6.18
.

The vector space
\mathscr{C}
consists of continuous functions
\phi\,:\,\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\mathscr{B}
such that the following norm

\|\phi\|_{\mathscr{C}}:=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}\|\phi(x)\|_{\mathscr{B}}

(6.27)

is finite. For
\phi\in\mathscr{C}
we define
\|\phi\|_{\tilde{\mathscr{C}}}:=\|\tilde{w}\,\phi\|_{\mathscr{C}}
, where
\tilde{w}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
is defined by the equality
\tilde{w}(x)=(1+|x|)^{-1/2}
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

Definition 6.19
.

We denote by
(\Delta_{i})_{i\in\{-1,0,1,\ldots\}}
the Littlewood-Paley blocks on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
. For
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
the Besov space
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
consists of linear and continuous maps

\phi\,:\,\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\to\mathscr{B}

(6.28)

such that the following norm

\|\phi\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}}:=\sup_{i\in\{-1,0,1,\ldots\}}2^{\alpha i}\,\|\Delta_{i}\phi\|_{\mathscr{C}}

(6.29)

is finite. For
\phi\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
we define

\|\phi\|_{\tilde{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}}\equiv\|\phi\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\tilde{w})}:=\sup_{i\in\{-1,0,1,\ldots\}}2^{\alpha i}\,\|\tilde{w}\,(\Delta_{i}\phi)\|_{\mathscr{C}}.

(6.30)

Lemma 6.20
.

There exists
C\in(0,\infty)
such that for all
\lambda\in(0,1]
,
\tau,\varepsilon\in[0,1]
,
t\in(0,1]
,
s\in(0,t]
,
\alpha\in(-\infty,-1/2)
and
a\in\mathbb{A}=\{0,1,2\}^{2}
,
\sigma\in\mathbb{G}
it holds

(A)

\|\partial^{a}\varPsi^{\sigma}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}\|_{\mathscr{C}}\leq C\,s^{-1/2-|a|}
,

(B)

\|\tilde{w}(\partial^{a}\varPsi^{\sigma}_{t,s}-\partial^{a}\varPsi^{\sigma}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s})\|_{\mathscr{C}}\leq C\,\lambda_{\tau\vee\varepsilon}^{\kappa}\,\lambda_{s}^{-\kappa}\,s^{-1/2-|a|}
,

(C)

\varPsi^{\sigma}\in\mathscr{C}^{-1/2}
and
\lim_{\tau,\varepsilon\searrow 0}\|\varPsi^{\sigma}-\varPsi^{\sigma}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\|_{\tilde{\mathscr{C}}^{\alpha}}=0
,

(D)

\limsup_{i\to\infty}2^{-i/2}\|\Delta_{i}\varPsi^{\sigma}\|_{\mathscr{C}}>0
,

where
\lambda_{s}
is a function of
\lambda
introduced in Def.
2.2
.

Proof.

Suppose that
\sigma=(\pm,\varsigma)
for some
\varsigma\in\mathbb{F}
. By Remark
6.4
we obtain

\|\partial^{a}\varPsi^{\sigma}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}\|^{2}_{\mathscr{C}}=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}\|\partial^{a}\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}^{\pm,\varsigma}(x)\|_{\mathscr{B}}^{2}\leq 2\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}\sum_{\tilde{\varsigma}\in\mathbb{F}}\int_{s}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2}}|\partial^{a}G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u}(x-y)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}u.

(6.31)

Using Lemma
5.3
and the fact that for all
y\in\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2}=(-1/(2\tau),1/(2\tau))^{2}
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
such that
|n|_{\infty}\geq 2
it holds
|y+n/\tau|_{\infty}\geq|y|_{\infty}+|n|_{\infty}/(2\tau)
one shows that

\int_{\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2}}|\partial^{a}G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u}(x-y)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}_{\tau}}|\partial^{a}G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u}(y)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\\
\leq\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}_{\tau}}|\partial^{a}G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\varepsilon;u}(y+n/\tau)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\leq C\,u^{-2-2|a|}.

(6.32)

As a result, we conclude that there exists
C\in(0,\infty)
such that for all
\varepsilon\in[0,1]
,
t\in(0,1]
,
s\in(0,t]
and
a\in\mathbb{A}
,
\sigma\in\mathbb{G}
it holds

\|\partial^{a}\varPsi^{\sigma}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}\|_{\mathscr{C}}\leq C\,s^{-1/2-|a|}.

(6.33)

This proves Item (A). Next, we observe that

\|\partial^{a}\varPsi^{\sigma}_{t,s}-\partial^{a}\varPsi^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon;t,s}\|^{2}_{\mathscr{C}}=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}\|\partial^{a}\varPsi_{t,s}^{\pm,\varsigma}(x)-\partial^{a}\varPsi_{\varepsilon;t,s}^{\pm,\varsigma}(x)\|_{\mathscr{B}}^{2}\\
\leq 2\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}\sum_{\tilde{\varsigma}\in\mathbb{F}}\int_{s}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\partial^{a}G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{u}(x-y)-\partial^{a}G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\varepsilon;u}(x-y)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}u.

(6.34)

The integrand on the RHS of the above equality vanishes identically if
u\in[4\varepsilon,1]
by Remark
5.2
(C). As a result, by the bound (
6.33
) we obtain

\|\partial^{a}\varPsi^{\sigma}_{t,s}-\partial^{a}\varPsi^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon;t,s}\|_{\mathscr{C}}\leq C\,\lambda_{4\varepsilon}^{\kappa}\,\lambda_{s}^{-\kappa}\,s^{-1/2-|a|}.

(6.35)

Next, note that

\|\tilde{w}(\partial^{a}\varPsi^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon;t,s}-\partial^{a}\varPsi^{\sigma}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s})\|^{2}_{\mathscr{C}}=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{w}(x)^{2}\,\|\partial^{a}\varPsi_{\varepsilon;t,s}^{\pm,\varsigma}(x)-\partial^{a}\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}^{\pm,\varsigma}(x)\|_{\mathscr{B}}^{2}\\
\leq 2\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{w}(x)^{2}\sum_{\tilde{\varsigma}\in\mathbb{F}}\int_{s}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\partial^{a}G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\varepsilon;u}(x-y)-\partial^{a}G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u}(x-y)\,1_{\mathbb{T}^{2}_{\tau}}(y)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}u,

(6.36)

where
1_{\mathbb{T}^{2}_{\tau}}
is the characteristic function of the set
\mathbb{T}^{2}_{\tau}=(-1/(2\tau),1/(2\tau))
.
Observe that
\tilde{w}(x)^{2}\leq\tilde{w}(y)^{2}\,\tilde{w}(x-y)^{-2}
. Consequently, it holds

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{w}(x)^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\partial^{a}G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\varepsilon;u}(x-y)-\partial^{a}G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u}(x-y)\,1_{\mathbb{T}^{2}_{\tau}}(y)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\\
\leq\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}^{2}_{2\tau}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}_{\tau}}|\partial^{a}G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\varepsilon;u}(x-y)-\partial^{a}G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u}(x-y)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\\
+\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus\mathbb{T}^{2}_{2\tau}}\tilde{w}(x)^{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}_{\tau}}|\partial^{a}G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\varepsilon;u}(x-y)-\partial^{a}G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u}(x-y)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\\
+\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus\mathbb{T}^{2}_{\tau}}\tilde{w}(y)^{2}\,\tilde{w}(x-y)^{-2}\,|\partial^{a}G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\varepsilon;u}(x-y)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y.

(6.37)

Using the fact that
\tilde{w}(x)=(1+|x|)^{-1/2}
we obtain

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{w}(x)^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\partial^{a}G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\varepsilon;u}(x-y)-\partial^{a}G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u}(x-y)\,1_{\mathbb{T}^{2}_{\tau}}(y)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\\
\leq\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}^{2}_{2\tau}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}_{\tau}}|\partial^{a}G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\varepsilon;u}(x-y)-\partial^{a}G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u}(x-y)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\\
+(2\tau)\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}_{\tau}}|\partial^{a}G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\varepsilon;u}(y)-\partial^{a}G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u}(y)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\\
+\tau\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{w}(y)^{-2}\,|\partial^{a}G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\varepsilon;u}(y)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y.

(6.38)

Since for all
x\in\mathbb{T}^{2}_{2\tau}
,
y\in\mathbb{T}^{2}_{\tau}
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}
it holds
|x-y+n/\tau|_{\infty}\geq|n|_{\infty}/(4\tau)
by Lemma
5.3
we obtain that there exist
c,C\in(0,\infty)
such that for all
\varepsilon\in[0,1]
,
u\in(0,1]
and
a\in\mathbb{A}
,
\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}\in\mathbb{F}
it holds

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}^{2}_{2\tau}}\sup_{y\in\mathbb{T}^{2}_{\tau}}|\partial^{a}G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\varepsilon;u}(x-y)-\partial^{a}G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u}(x-y)|\leq C\,u^{-2-|a|}\exp(-c/\tau^{\zeta_{\flat}})

(6.39)

This implies the bound

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}^{2}_{2\tau}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}_{\tau}}|\partial^{a}G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\varepsilon;u}(x-y)-\partial^{a}G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\tau,\varepsilon;u}(x-y)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\leq C\,u^{-2-2|a|}

(6.40)

with a possibly different constant
C\in(0,\infty)
. Moreover, by Lemma
5.3
we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{w}(y)^{-2}\,|\partial^{a}G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{\varepsilon;u}(y)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\leq C\,u^{-2-2|a|}.

(6.41)

Consequently, we obtain

\|\partial^{a}\varPsi^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon;t,s}-\partial^{a}\varPsi^{\sigma}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}\|_{\mathscr{C}}\leq C\,\tau^{1/2}\,s^{-1/2-|a|}.

(6.42)

Item (B) follows now from the bounds (
6.35
), (
6.42
). Item (C) is a consequence of Items (A), (B) and the identity

\Delta_{i}\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon}=\Delta_{i}\varPhi_{\tau,\varepsilon;s,1}

(6.43)

valid for all
\tau,\varepsilon\in[0,1]
,
i\in\{-1,0,1,\ldots\}
and
s\in(0,c\,2^{-i})
, where
c\in(0,\infty)
is a universal constant depending on the convention used in defining the Littlewood-Paley blocks. To prove Item (D) we note that by Remark
6.4

\|\Delta_{i}\varPsi^{\sigma}\|^{2}_{\mathscr{C}}=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}\|\Delta_{i}\varPsi^{\pm,\varsigma}(x)\|_{\mathscr{B}}^{2}\geq\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}\sum_{\tilde{\varsigma}\in\mathbb{F}}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|(\Delta_{i}\Delta_{i}G^{\pm;\varsigma,\tilde{\varsigma}}_{u})(x-y)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}u

(6.44)

and express the RHS of the above bound as an integral in the frequency space. This finishes the proof.
∎

7
Effective potential and Polchinski equation

In this section we introduce the notion of an effective potential and prove that a solution of the mild form of the Polchinski equation (
7.13
) satisfies the equation for an effective potential. A solution
U_{\tau,\varepsilon;{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}}
of the Polchinski equation will be constructed in Sec.
14
in terms of the fixed point
X_{\tau,\varepsilon;{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}}
of the map
\mathbf{X}_{\tau,\varepsilon;{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}}
constructed in Sec.
13
. Then by the results of this section such
U_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}
is an effective potential at the scale
t
. Using this fact in Sec.
15
we will show that
U_{\tau,\varepsilon;t=0}
is the generating functional of the connected amputated Schwinger functions.

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
\tau,\varepsilon\in(0,1]
. Suppose that
\mathbf{E}\exp(U_{\tau,\varepsilon}(\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon}))\neq 0
. The interacting measure satisfies the equality

\mu_{\tau,\varepsilon}(F)=\frac{\mathbf{E}(F(\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon})\exp(U_{\tau,\varepsilon}(\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon})))}{\mathbf{E}\exp(U_{\tau,\varepsilon}(\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon}))}\in\mathbb{C}

(7.1)

for all functionals
F\in\mathscr{N}(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}})
.

Proof.

The lemma is a consequence of Def.
4.16
of the measure
\mu_{\tau,\varepsilon}
and Lemma
6.15
.
∎

Definition 7.2
.

Let
\tau,\varepsilon\in(0,1]
and
U_{\tau,\varepsilon}\equiv U_{\tau}(g_{\tau,\varepsilon},r_{\tau,\varepsilon})\in\mathscr{N}(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}})
be defined as specified in Def.
4.16
. We call a functional
U_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}\in\mathscr{N}(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}})
an effective potential at the spatial scale
t\in[0,1]
(of the Gross-Neveu model with the parameters
g_{\tau,\varepsilon},r_{\tau,\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{R}
) if it satisfies the following equality

\exp(U_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}(\phi))=\mathbf{E}\exp(U_{\tau,\varepsilon}(\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,0}+\phi))\in\mathscr{G}

(7.2)

for all
\phi\in\mathcal{C}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\otimes\mathscr{G}^{-}
, where
\mathcal{C}_{\tau,\varepsilon}=(\mathrm{Span}\{x\mapsto\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}p\cdot x}\,|\,p\in\Lambda_{\tau,\varepsilon}\})^{\mathbb{G}}\otimes\mathscr{G}^{-}\subset C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}}\otimes\mathscr{G}^{-}
was introduced in Def.
4.5
and
\mathscr{G}
is an infinite-dimensional Grassmann algebra.

Remark 7.3
.

Note that for
s\leq t
it holds

\exp(U_{\tau,\varepsilon;s}(\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}+\phi))=\mathbf{E}_{s}\exp(U_{\tau,\varepsilon}(\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,0}+\phi)).

(7.3)

Consequently, for
s\leq t
an effective potential fulfills the following identity

\exp(U_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}(\phi))=\mathbf{E}\exp(U_{\tau,\varepsilon;s}(\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}+\phi)).

(7.4)

Remark 7.4
.

For all
\tau,\varepsilon,t\in(0,1]
we have

\dot{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}({\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}-{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}})\in\mathcal{C}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\otimes\mathcal{C}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\subset C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}),

(7.5)

where the function
G_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}({\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}-{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}})\in C(\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
coincides with the map
(x,y)\mapsto G_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}(x-y)
.

Lemma 7.5
.

Let
\tau,\varepsilon\in(0,1]
and
\mathscr{G}
be an infinite-dimensional Grassmann algebra. Suppose that the function
[0,1]\ni t\mapsto U_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}\in\mathscr{N}(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}})
satisfies the Polchinski flow equation

\partial_{t}U_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}(\phi)=-\frac{1}{2}\langle\mathrm{D}_{\phi}^{2}U_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}(\phi),\dot{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}({\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}-{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}})\rangle_{\tau}+\frac{1}{2}\langle\mathrm{D}_{\phi}U_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}(\phi),\dot{G}_{\varepsilon;t}\ast\mathrm{D}_{\phi}U_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}(\phi)\rangle_{\tau}

(7.6)

with the initial condition
U_{\tau,\varepsilon;0}(\phi)=U_{\tau,\varepsilon}(\phi)
for all
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2}_{\tau})^{\mathbb{G}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}^{-}
. Then for every
t\in[0,1]
the functional
U_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}\in\mathscr{N}(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}})
is an effective potential at the spatial scale
t
.

Proof.

Let us first observe that for every
U\in\mathscr{N}(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}})
such that
U(\phi)\in\mathscr{G}^{+}
for all
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2}_{\tau})^{\mathbb{G}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}^{-}
it holds

\begin{gathered}\mathrm{D}_{\phi}\exp(U(\phi))=\exp(U(\phi))\,\mathrm{D}_{\phi}U(\phi)\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}^{-},\\
\mathrm{D}_{\phi}^{2}\exp(U(\phi))=\exp(U(\phi))\,\mathrm{D}^{2}_{\phi}U(\phi)-\exp(U(\phi))\,(\mathrm{D}_{\phi}U(\phi)\otimes\mathrm{D}_{\phi}U(\phi))\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{4})^{\mathbb{G}^{2}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}^{+}.\end{gathered}

(7.7)

In consequence, the function
[0,1]\ni t\mapsto U_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}\in\mathscr{N}(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}})
satisfies the Polchinski flow equation (
7.6
) for all
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2}_{\tau})^{\mathbb{G}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}^{-}
if and only if it satisfies the equation

\partial_{t}\exp(U_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}(\phi))=-\frac{1}{2}\langle\mathrm{D}_{\phi}^{2}\exp(U_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}(\phi)),\dot{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}({\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}-{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}})\rangle_{\tau}

(7.8)

for all
t\in(0,1]
and
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2}_{\tau})^{\mathbb{G}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}^{-}
. Note that by Lemma
6.17
the equation

\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}\exp(U_{\tau,\varepsilon}(\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,0}+\phi))=-\frac{1}{2}\langle\mathrm{D}_{\phi}^{2}\mathbf{E}\exp(U_{\tau,\varepsilon}(\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,0}+\phi)),\dot{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}({\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}-{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}})\rangle_{\tau}

(7.9)

holds true for all
t\in(0,1]
and
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2}_{\tau})^{\mathbb{G}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}^{-}
. The statement follows now from Lemma
7.6
.
∎

Lemma 7.6
.

Let
\tau,\varepsilon\in(0,1]
and
\mathscr{G}
be an infinite-dimensional Grassmann algebra. Suppose that the function
[0,1]\ni t\mapsto Z_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}\in\mathscr{N}(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}})
satisfies the equation

\partial_{t}Z_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}(\phi)=-\frac{1}{2}\langle\mathrm{D}_{\phi}^{2}Z_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}(\phi),\dot{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}({\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}-{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}})\rangle_{\tau}

(7.10)

with the initial condition
Z_{\tau,\varepsilon;0}(\phi)=0
for all
\phi\in\mathcal{C}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\otimes\mathscr{G}^{-}
. Then
Z_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}(\phi)=0
for all
t\in[0,1]
and
\phi\in\mathcal{C}_{\tau,\varepsilon}\otimes\mathscr{G}^{-}
.

Proof.

Recall that a functional
Z_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}\in\mathscr{N}(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}})
is a collection
Z_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}=(Z_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}^{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
such that
Z_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}^{0}\in\mathbb{C}
and
Z_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}^{m}\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2m})^{\mathbb{G}^{m}}
is antisymmetric for
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
. Next, set
m_{\tau,\varepsilon}:=|\Lambda_{\tau,\varepsilon}||\mathbb{G}|=\dim(\mathcal{C}_{\tau,\varepsilon})
and let
\{e_{1},\ldots,e_{m_{\tau,\varepsilon}}\}
be a basis of
\mathcal{C}_{\tau,\varepsilon}
. For
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
i_{1},\ldots,i_{m}\in\{1,\ldots,m_{\tau,\varepsilon}\}
define

Z^{(i_{1},\ldots,i_{m})}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}:=\frac{1}{m!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{m}}(-1)^{\mathrm{sgn}(\pi)}\langle Z^{m}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t},e_{\pi(i_{1})}\otimes\ldots\otimes e_{\pi(i_{m})}\rangle\in\mathbb{C},

(7.11)

where
\mathcal{P}_{m}
is the set of permutations of
\{1,\ldots,m\}
. Observe that
Z^{(i_{1},\ldots,i_{m})}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}
vanishes identically if
m>m_{\tau,\varepsilon}
. Hence, by Remark
7.4
Eq. (
7.10
) implies that the finite collection

\{Z^{0}_{\tau,\varepsilon;{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}}\}\cup\{Z^{(i_{1},\ldots,i_{m})}_{\tau,\varepsilon;{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}}\,|\,m,i_{1},\ldots,i_{m}\in\{1,\ldots,m_{\tau,\varepsilon}\}\}

(7.12)

satisfies a first order linear ODE with a trivial boundary condition. This proves the claim.
∎

Lemma 7.7
.

Let
\tau,\varepsilon\in(0,1]
and
\mathscr{G}
be an infinite-dimensional Grassmann algebra. Suppose that a continuous function
[0,1]\ni t\mapsto U_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}\in\mathscr{N}(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}})
satisfies the integral form of the Polchinski equation

U_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}(\phi)=\mathbf{E}U_{\tau,\varepsilon}(\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,0}+\phi)\\
+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\mathbf{E}\langle\mathrm{D}_{\phi}U_{\tau,\varepsilon;s}(\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}+\phi),\dot{G}_{\varepsilon;s}\ast\mathrm{D}_{\phi}U_{\tau,\varepsilon;s}(\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}+\phi)\rangle_{\tau}\,\mathrm{d}s

(7.13)

for all
\phi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2}_{\tau})^{\mathbb{G}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}^{-}
. Then for every
t\in[0,1]
the functional
U_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}\in\mathscr{N}(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}})
is an effective potential at the spatial scale
t
.

Proof.

It holds

\partial_{t}U_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}(\phi)=\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}U_{\tau,\varepsilon;0}(\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,0}+\phi)\\
+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}\langle\mathrm{D}_{\phi}U_{\tau,\varepsilon;s}(\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}+\phi),\dot{G}_{\varepsilon;s}\ast\mathrm{D}_{\phi}U_{\tau,\varepsilon;s}(\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}+\phi)\rangle_{\tau}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
+\frac{1}{2}\langle\mathrm{D}_{\phi}U_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}(\phi),\dot{G}_{\varepsilon;t}\ast\mathrm{D}_{\phi}U_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}(\phi)\rangle_{\tau}.

(7.14)

Lemma
6.17
implies that

\partial_{t}\mathbf{E}F(\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}+\phi)=-\frac{1}{2}\langle\mathrm{D}_{\phi}^{2}F(\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}+\phi),\dot{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}({\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}-{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}})\rangle_{\tau}

(7.15)

for all
F\in\mathscr{N}(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2}_{\tau}))^{\mathbb{G}}
. Hence, we obtain

\partial_{t}U_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}(\phi)=-\frac{1}{2}\langle\mathrm{D}_{\phi}^{2}\mathbf{E}U_{\tau,\varepsilon;0}(\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,0}+\phi),\dot{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}({\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}-{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}})\rangle_{\tau}\\
-\frac{1}{4}\int_{0}^{t}\langle\mathrm{D}_{\phi}^{2}\mathbf{E}\langle\mathrm{D}_{\phi}U_{\tau,\varepsilon;s}(\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}+\phi),\dot{G}_{\varepsilon;s}\ast\mathrm{D}_{\phi}U_{\tau,\varepsilon;s}(\varPsi_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}+\phi)\rangle_{\tau},\dot{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}({\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}-{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}})\rangle_{\tau}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
+\frac{1}{2}\langle\mathrm{D}_{\phi}U_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}(\phi),\dot{G}_{\varepsilon;t}\ast\mathrm{D}_{\phi}U_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}(\phi)\rangle_{\tau}.

(7.16)

Using Eq. (
7.13
) the sum of the first two terms on the RHS of the above equation can be rewritten as

-\frac{1}{2}\langle\mathrm{D}_{\phi}^{2}U_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}(\phi),\dot{G}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}({\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}-{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}})\rangle_{\tau}.

(7.17)

This implies that the function
[0,1]\ni t\mapsto U_{\tau,\varepsilon;t}\in\mathscr{N}(C^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}})
satisfies the Polchinski flow equation (
7.6
) with the initial condition
U_{\tau,\varepsilon;0}(\phi)=U_{\tau,\varepsilon}(\phi)
. The statement follows now from Lemma
7.5
.
∎

8
Symmetries

In this section we discuss the symmetries of the Gross-Neveu model that are instrumental in the decomposition of kernels of functionals into the local part and the remainder, which is defined in Sec.
11
. As we mentioned in Sec.
2
the form of the local terms that appear in this decomposition is restricted by the symmetries of the kernels.

Definition 8.1
.

The jet prolongation of
\varphi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}}
is defined by

\mathbf{J}\,:\,C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}}\to C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{\mathbb{A}\times\mathbb{G}},\qquad(\mathbf{J}\varphi)^{a,\sigma}\equiv\mathbf{J}\varphi^{a,\sigma}=\partial^{a}\varphi^{\sigma},\quad a\in\mathbb{A},~\sigma\in\mathbb{G}.

(8.1)

Definition 8.2

(Symmetries of plane)
.

Let
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and let
R\,:\,\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
be a matrix of a rotation around the origin or a reflection with respect to a line passing through the origin. For every
R=(R^{jk})_{j,k\in\{1,2\}}
as above we choose a complex invertible
2\times 2
matrix
\gamma(R)
such that

\gamma(R)^{-1}\,\gamma_{j}\,\gamma(R)=\sum_{k\in\{1,2\}}R^{jk}\gamma_{k},

where
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}
are the gamma matrices introduced in Def.
4.2
. We set
\Gamma(R)=\gamma(R)^{\oplus N}
. For
\varphi\equiv(\bar{\varphi},\underaccent{\bar}{\varphi})\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}}
we define

\mathbf{T}(x,R)\varphi\equiv(\mathbf{T}(x,R)\bar{\varphi},\mathbf{T}(x,R)\bar{\varphi})\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}}

(8.2)

by the equalities

(\mathbf{T}(x,R)\bar{\varphi})(y):=\Gamma(R^{-1})^{\mathrm{t}}\bar{\varphi}(R^{-1}(y-x)),\qquad(\mathbf{T}(x,R)\underaccent{\bar}{\varphi})(y):=\Gamma(R)\underaccent{\bar}{\varphi}(R^{-1}(y-x))

(8.3)

for all
y\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Let
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
. We say that a Schwartz distribution
V\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{2m})^{\mathbb{G}^{m}}
is invariant under the symmetries of the plane iff

\langle V,\varphi_{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes\varphi_{m}\rangle=\langle V,\mathbf{T}(x,R)\varphi_{1}\otimes\ldots\mathbf{T}(x,R)\varphi_{m}\rangle

(8.4)

for all
\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{m}\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}}
, all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and all
R
as above. The action of
\mathbf{T}(x,R)
on
\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{\mathbb{A}\times\mathbb{G}}
is defined in such a way that

\mathbf{T}(x,R)(\mathbf{J}\varphi):=\mathbf{J}(\mathbf{T}(x,R)\varphi)

(8.5)

for all
\varphi\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}}
, where
\mathbf{J}
is the jet prolongation introduced in Definition
8.1
. We say that a Schwartz distribution
V\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{2m})^{\mathbb{A}^{m}\times\mathbb{G}^{m}}
is invariant under the symmetries of the plane iff the condition (
8.4
) is satisfied for all
\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{m}\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{\mathbb{A}\times\mathbb{G}}
, all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and all
R
as above.

Remark 8.3
.

It is possible to choose the assignment
R\mapsto\gamma(R)
such that
(x,R)\mapsto\mathbf{T}(x,R)
is a projective representation of the Euclidean group on
\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}}
. The choice of the assignment
R\mapsto\gamma(R)
does not play a role in what follows.

Definition 8.4

(Symmetries of torus)
.

A Schwartz distribution
V\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{2m})^{\mathbb{A}^{m}\times\mathbb{G}^{m}}
is invariant under the symmetries of the torus iff the condition (
8.4
) is satisfied for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and all
R\,:\,\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
that are a rotation by a multiple of
\pi/2
or a reflection with respect to the line
x^{1}=0
or
x^{2}=0
.

Definition 8.5

(Internal rotations)
.

Let
\varphi\equiv(\bar{\varphi},\underaccent{\bar}{\varphi})\equiv(\varphi^{-},\varphi^{+})\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{\mathbb{A}\times\mathbb{G}}
and a permutation
\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{N}
. We define
\mathbf{T}(\pi)\varphi\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}}
by the equality

(\mathbf{T}(\pi)\varphi)^{a,(\pm,n,\alpha)}(x):=\varphi^{a,(\pm,\pi(n),\alpha)}(x)

(8.6)

for all
a\in\mathbb{A}
,
n\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
,
\alpha\in\{1,2\}
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Let
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
. We say that a Schwartz distribution
V\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{2m})^{\mathbb{A}^{m}\times\mathbb{G}^{m}}
is invariant under the internal symmetries iff

\langle V,\varphi_{1}\otimes\ldots\varphi_{m}\rangle=\langle V,\mathbf{T}(\pi)\varphi_{1}\otimes\ldots\mathbf{T}(\pi)\varphi_{m}\rangle

(8.7)

for all
\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{N}
and all
\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{m}\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{\mathbb{A}\times\mathbb{G}}
.

Definition 8.6

(Charge conjugation)
.

For
\varphi\equiv(\bar{\varphi},\underaccent{\bar}{\varphi})\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{\mathbb{A}\times\mathbb{G}}
we define we define

\mathbf{C}\varphi\equiv(\mathbf{C}\bar{\varphi},\mathbf{C}\bar{\varphi})\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}}

by the equalities

(\mathbf{C}\bar{\varphi})^{a}(x)=\Gamma_{2}\underaccent{\bar}{\varphi}^{a}(x),\qquad(\mathbf{C}\underaccent{\bar}{\varphi})^{a}(x)=\Gamma_{2}\bar{\varphi}^{a}(x)

(8.8)

for
a\in\mathbb{A}
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Let
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
. We say that a distribution
V\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{2m})^{\mathbb{A}^{m}\times\mathbb{G}^{m}}
is invariant under the charge conjugation symmetry iff

\langle V,\varphi_{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes\varphi_{m}\rangle=\langle V,\mathbf{C}\varphi_{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes\mathbf{C}\varphi_{m}\rangle

(8.9)

for all
\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{m}\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}}
.

Remark 8.7
.

One shows that if
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\setminus 2\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
V\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{2m})^{\mathbb{A}^{m}\times\mathbb{G}^{m}}
is invariant under the charge conjugation symmetry, then
V=0
.

Remark 8.8
.

The only local functionals of degree two in
\psi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}
with up to one derivative compatible with all of the above symmetries are of the form

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\bar{\psi}(x)\cdot\underaccent{\bar}{\psi}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x,\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\bar{\psi}(x)\cdot((\Gamma_{1}\partial_{1}+\Gamma_{2}\partial_{2})\underaccent{\bar}{\psi})(x)\,\mathrm{d}x.

(8.10)

The only local functionals of degree four in
\psi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}
without derivatives compatible with all of the above symmetries are of the form

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\bar{\psi}(x)\cdot\underaccent{\bar}{\psi}(x))^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x,\quad\sum_{j\in\{1,2\}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\bar{\psi}(x)\cdot\Gamma_{j}\underaccent{\bar}{\psi}(x))^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x,\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\bar{\psi}(x)\cdot\Gamma_{1}\Gamma_{2}\underaccent{\bar}{\psi}(x))^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x.

(8.11)

For the proof of the above claims we refer the reader to
[
MW73
, Appendix]
or
[
DY23
, Appendix C]
.

9
Weights

In this section we define the weights that are used in the definitions of various norms and establish some of their properties. The use of weights growing stretched exponentially allows to prove stretched exponential decay of truncated correlations. This choice of the weights will also play a role in the proof of the estimates for the map
\mathbf{R}
presented in Sec.
11
.

Definition 9.1
.

The diameter of the set of points
\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}\}\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
is defined by

\mathrm{D}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}):=\max_{i,j\in\{1,\ldots,m\}}|x_{i}-x_{j}|.

(9.1)

Let
\zeta:=4/5
. For
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
\nu\in[0,1/2]
and
t\in(0,1]
we define the weights
w_{\nu}\in C(\mathbb{R}^{2})
and
w^{m}_{t;\nu}\in C(\mathbb{R}^{2m})
by
w_{\nu}(x)=(1+|x|)^{-\nu}
and

w^{m}_{t;\nu}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}):=(1+|x_{1}|)^{-\nu}(1+\mathrm{D}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}))^{1/2-\nu}\exp(t^{-\zeta}\mathrm{D}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})^{\zeta}).

(9.2)

We set
\tilde{w}:=w_{1/2}
,
w^{m}_{t}:=w^{m}_{t;0}
,
\tilde{w}^{m}_{t}:=w^{m}_{t;1/2}
and
\zeta_{\star}:=-2(1/\zeta-7/8)=-3/4
.

Remark 9.2
.

Note that the upper index
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
of
w^{m}_{t;\nu}
does not denote the power.

Remark 9.3
.

We will frequently use the fact that
\tilde{w}^{m}_{t}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})\leq w^{m}_{t}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})
for all
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
t\in(0,1]
and
x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
. The weights
\tilde{w}^{m}_{t}
will be used to study the infinite volume limit. Observe that the weight
\tilde{w}
appeared already in Lemma
6.20
.

Remark 9.4
.

It holds
1/\tilde{w}(x)\leq 1/\tilde{w}(y)\,1/\tilde{w}(x-y)
for all
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

Lemma 9.5
.

There exists
C\in(0,\infty)
such that for all
l\in\{0,1\}
,
\nu\in[0,1/2]
,
t\in(0,1]
,
s\in(0,t)
,
k\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
and
x_{1},\ldots,x_{m},y_{1},\ldots,y_{m},y,z\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
it holds

(a)

w^{m}_{t;\nu}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})\leq w^{k+1}_{t;\nu}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k},y)\,w^{2}_{t}(y,z)\,w^{m-k+1}_{t}(z,x_{k+1},\ldots,x_{m}),

(b)

w^{m}_{t;\nu}(x_{m},\ldots,x_{1})\leq w^{m-k+1}_{t}(z,x_{m},\ldots,x_{k+1})\,w^{2}_{t}(y,z)\,w^{k+1}_{t;\nu}(y,x_{k},\ldots,x_{1}),

(c)

w^{m}_{t;\nu}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})\leq w^{m}_{t;\nu}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})\,w^{2}_{t}(y_{1},x_{1})\ldots w^{2}_{t}(y_{m},x_{m}),

(d)

w^{k}_{t;\nu}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k})\leq w^{m}_{t;\nu}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k},x_{k+1},\ldots,x_{m}),

(e)

w^{k}_{t;\nu}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k})\leq w^{m}_{t}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k},x_{k+1},\ldots,x_{m})w_{\nu}(x_{m}),

(f)

\int_{0}^{1}(1-u)^{l}\mathrm{D}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})^{l+1}w^{m}_{t;\nu}(ux_{1},\ldots,ux_{m})\,\mathrm{d}u\leq C\,s^{l+1}\,(1-s/t)^{\zeta_{\star}}\,w^{m}_{s;\nu}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})
.

Remark 9.6
.

The above lemma will be only used with
\nu\in\{0,1/2\}
.

Proof.

Items (a) and (b) follow from the bounds

\begin{gathered}\mathrm{D}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})\leq\mathrm{D}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{k},y)+|y-z|+\mathrm{D}(z,x_{k+1},\ldots,x_{m}),\\
|y|\leq|y-z|+\mathrm{D}(z,x_{k+1},\ldots,x_{m})+|x_{m}|,\end{gathered}

(9.3)

which are consequences of the triangle inequality, and the bounds

(a+b+c)^{\mu}\leq a^{\mu}+b^{\mu}+c^{\mu},\qquad(1+a+b+c)^{\mu}\leq(1+a)^{\mu}(1+b)^{\mu}(1+c)^{\mu}

(9.4)

valid for
\mu\in(0,1]
and
a,b,c\in[0,\infty)
. Item (c) is proved along the same lines with the use of the bounds

\mathrm{D}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})\leq\mathrm{D}(y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})+|y_{1}-x_{1}|+\ldots+|y_{m}-x_{m}|,\qquad|y_{1}|\leq|y_{1}-x_{1}|+|x_{1}|.

(9.5)

To prove Item (d) one uses the fact that the function

[0,\infty)\ni d\mapsto(1+d)^{1/2-\nu}\,\exp(t^{-\zeta}d^{\zeta})\in\mathbb{R}

(9.6)

is monotonic. Item (e) follows from Item (d), the bound

|x_{m}|\leq|x_{1}|+\mathrm{D}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})
and the inequality
(1+a+b)^{\mu}\leq(1+a)^{\mu}(1+b)^{\mu}
valid for
\mu\in(0,1]
and
a,b\in[0,\infty)
. We proceed to the proof of Item (f). Let
\mathrm{D}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})=d
. Observe that it holds

d^{l+1}\frac{w^{m;\nu}_{t}(ux_{1},\ldots,ux_{m})}{w^{m;\nu}_{s}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})}=\frac{(1+|x_{1}|)^{\nu}}{(1+u\,|x_{1}|)^{\nu}}\frac{(1+u\,d)^{1/2-\nu}}{(1+d)^{1/2-\nu}}d^{l+1}\exp((u^{\zeta}t^{-\zeta}-s^{-\zeta})d^{\zeta})\\
\leq u^{-\nu}d^{l+1}\exp((u^{\zeta}t^{-\zeta}-s^{-\zeta})d^{\zeta})\leq\hat{C}\,u^{-\nu}\,(s^{-\zeta}-u^{\zeta}t^{-\zeta})^{-(l+1)/\zeta},

(9.7)

where
\hat{C}\in(0,\infty)
is such that

d^{l+1}\exp(-d^{\zeta}r)\leq\hat{C}\,r^{-(l+1)/\zeta}

for all
l\in\{0,1\}
,
r\in(0,\infty)
and
\zeta\in(0,1]
. Consequently, it holds

\int_{0}^{1}(1-u)^{l}\mathrm{D}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})^{l+1}\frac{w^{m;\nu}_{t}(ux_{1},\ldots,ux_{m})}{w^{m;\nu}_{s}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})}\,\mathrm{d}u\leq\hat{C}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{s^{l+1}\,u^{-\nu}\,(1-u)^{l}}{(1-u^{\zeta}(s/t)^{\zeta})^{(l+1)/\zeta}}\,\mathrm{d}u

(9.8)

Note that for
0<s<t\leq 1
and
0<u\leq 1
it holds
1-u^{\zeta}\geq\zeta(1-u)
and

(1-u^{\zeta}(s/t)^{\zeta})^{(l+1)/\zeta}\geq\zeta^{(l+1)/\zeta}\,(1-u(s/t))^{(l+1)/\zeta}\\
\geq\zeta^{(l+1)/\zeta}\,(1-u)^{\zeta_{\sharp}(l)}\,(1-s/t)^{(l+1)(1/\zeta-7/8)},

(9.9)

where
\zeta_{\sharp}(l):=(l+1)/\zeta-(l+1)(1/\zeta-7/8)>0
. Since
l\in\{0,1\}
and
l-\zeta_{\sharp}(l)>-1
the bound stated in Item (f) holds true with

C=\max_{l\in\{0,1\}}\zeta^{-2/\zeta}\,\hat{C}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{u^{-1/2}\,(1-u)^{l}}{(1-u)^{\zeta_{\sharp}(l)}}\,\mathrm{d}u<\infty.

(9.10)

This finishes the proof.
∎

10
Spaces of kernels and functionals

In this section we define the spaces of kernels and functionals in infinite volume that are used in Sec.
13
to solve the system of equations (
2.38
), (
2.48
),
(
2.32
), (
2.33
),
(
2.34
) introduced in Sec.
2
using the Banach fixed point theorem. We also define the map
\mathbf{A}_{\tau,\varepsilon;t,s}
and analyze its properties. We refer the reader to Sec.
2
for the motivation behind the definitions stated in this section.

Definition 10.1
.

Let
m,n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
\mathscr{A}
be a unital Banach algebra. We denote by
\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2m},\mathscr{A})
the space of Schwartz test functions valued in
\mathscr{A}
equipped with the usual family of semi-norms. The space of
\mathscr{A}
-valued Schwartz distributions is denoted by
\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{2m},\mathscr{A})
and by definition coincides with the space of continuous maps
\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2m})\to\mathscr{A}
equipped with the topology of pointwise convergence. We denote by

\langle{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}},{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\rangle\,:\,\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{2m},\mathscr{A})\times\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2m},\mathscr{A})\to\mathscr{A}

the unique bilinear map such that
\langle V,\varphi A\rangle:=\langle V,\varphi\rangle\,A
for all
V\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{2m},\mathscr{A})
,
\varphi\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2m})
and
A\in\mathscr{A}
, where
\langle V,\varphi\rangle
is the standard paring between a distribution and a test function. We denote by

{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\otimes{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\,:\,\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2m},\mathscr{A})\times\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2n},\mathscr{A})\to\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{m+n},\mathscr{A})

the unique bilinear map such that
\varphi A\otimes\psi B:=(\varphi\otimes\psi)\,AB
for all
\varphi\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2m})
,
\psi\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2n})
and
A,B\in\mathscr{A}
, where
\varphi\otimes\psi\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2m+2n})
is the standard tensor product of Schwartz functions. Let
\mathbb{K}
be a finite set. The maps

\begin{gathered}\langle{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}},{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\rangle\,:\,\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{2m},\mathscr{A})^{\mathbb{K}}\times\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2m},\mathscr{A})^{\mathbb{K}}\to\mathscr{A},\\
{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\otimes{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\,:\,\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2m},\mathscr{A})^{\mathbb{K}^{m}}\times\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2n},\mathscr{A})^{\mathbb{K}^{n}}\to\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2m+2n},\mathscr{A})^{\mathbb{K}^{m+n}}\end{gathered}

(10.1)

are defined in analogy to Def.
3.7
.

Remark 10.2
.

In what follows, we assume that
\mathscr{G}
is an infinite dimensional Grassmann algebra. It is important
\mathscr{G}
is infinite dimensional as we shall frequently use the fact that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
there exist
g_{1},\ldots,g_{n}\in\mathscr{G}
such that
g_{1}\ldots g_{n}\neq 0
. We do not equip
\mathscr{G}
with any topology.

Remark 10.3
.

Let
\mathscr{A}=\mathscr{A}^{+}\oplus\mathscr{A}^{-}
be a graded unital algebra. We define
\mathscr{A}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}
to be the algebraic graded tensor product of graded algebras
\mathscr{A}
and
\mathscr{G}
. In particular, the product in
\mathscr{A}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}
satisfies the condition
(A\otimes g)(B\otimes h)=(-1)^{\deg(g)\deg(B)}\,AB\otimes gh
for all
A,B\in\mathscr{A}
and
gh\in\mathscr{G}
of definite parity. We identify
A\in\mathscr{A}
and
g\in\mathscr{G}
with

A\otimes 1\in\mathscr{A}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}\qquad\mathrm{and}\qquad\mathds{1}\otimes g\in\mathscr{A}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G},

(10.2)

respectively. We use these identifications to make sense of
A+g,Ag\in\mathscr{A}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}
. Let
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
\mathbb{K}
be a finite set. We define the paring

\langle{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}},{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\rangle\,:\,\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{2m},\mathscr{A})^{\mathbb{K}^{m}}\times(\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2m},\mathscr{A})^{\mathbb{K}^{m}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G})\to\mathscr{A}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}

(10.3)

as the unique bilinear map such that
\langle V,\varphi\otimes g\rangle:=\langle V,\varphi\rangle\,\otimes g
for all
V\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{2m},\mathscr{A})^{\mathbb{K}}
,
\varphi\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2m})^{\mathbb{K}}
and
g\in\mathscr{G}
. We denote by

{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\otimes{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\displaystyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\textstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.7}{\hskip 0.28453pt$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$\hskip 0.28453pt}}}}}}\,:\,(\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2m},\mathscr{A})^{\mathbb{K}^{m}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G})\times(\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2n},\mathscr{A})^{\mathbb{K}^{n}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G})\to\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2m+2n},\mathscr{A})^{\mathbb{K}^{m+n}}\otimes_{\mathrm{alg}}\mathscr{G}

(10.4)

the unique bilinear map such that
(\varphi\otimes g)\otimes(\psi\otimes h):=(-1)^{\deg(g)\deg(\psi)}(\varphi\otimes\psi)\otimes gh
for all
\varphi\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2m},\mathscr{A})^{\mathbb{K}^{m}}
,
\psi\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2n},\mathscr{A})^{\mathbb{K}^{n}}
and
g,h\in\mathscr{G}
of definite parity.

Definition 10.4
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
\mathscr{A}
be a unital Banach algebra. We denote by
\mathcal{P}_{m}
the group of permutations of the set
\{1,\ldots,m\}
. The antisymmetric part of a distribution
V\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{2m},\mathscr{A})^{\mathbb{A}^{m}\times\mathbb{G}^{m}}
is the distribution
\mathbf{S}V\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{2m},\mathscr{A})^{\mathbb{A}^{m}\times\mathbb{G}^{m}}
defined by

\langle\mathbf{S}V,\varphi_{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes\varphi_{m}\rangle=\frac{1}{m!}\sum_{\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{m}}(-1)^{\mathrm{sgn}(\pi)}\langle V,\varphi_{\pi(1)}\otimes\ldots\otimes\varphi_{\pi(m)}\rangle

(10.5)

for all
\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{m}\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{\mathbb{A}\times\mathbb{G}}
. We say that a distribution
V\in\mathscr{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{2m},\mathscr{A})^{\mathbb{A}^{m}\times\mathbb{G}^{m}}
is antisymmetric iff
V=\mathbf{S}V
.

Remark 10.5
.

Recall that
\mathscr{B}=\mathscr{B}(\mathscr{H})
is the algebra of bounded operators acting on the Hilbert space
\mathscr{H}
introduced in Def.
6.2

Definition 10.6
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
\mathscr{A}
be a unital Banach subalgebra of
\mathscr{B}
. We denote by
\mathscr{M}^{m}(\mathscr{A})
